<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra generates a completely rational conformal net. All its simple grading-restricted modules are unitarizable, its canonical fusion forms are positive, and the Carpi–Weiner–Xu functor gives a braided unitary tensor equivalence from its grading-restricted finite-length module category onto the finite-index sectors of the net. Gui's extension theorems then identify normalized irreducible finite-index local extensions of these nets with simple CFT-type conformal extensions of the vertex operator algebras.

- **claim-main-correspondence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra over $\mathbb C$. Then:* 1. *$V$ has polynomial energy bounds and is CKLW-strongly local. The algebras <a href="#eq:intro-net" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-net">[eq:intro-net]</a> form an irreducible conformal net $A_V$.* 2. *$V$ is completely unitary: every simple grading-restricted module is unitarizable, and all canonical invariant Hermitian fusion forms are positive definite.* 3. *Every unitary grading-restricted finite-length module is strongly integrable, with the closed-field equality <a href="#eq:intro-cwx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-cwx">[eq:intro-cwx]</a>.* 4. *The net $A_V$ is completely rational.* 5. *After choosing invariant positive forms on the simple modules, the Carpi–Weiner–Xu functor gives a braided unitary tensor equivalence $$\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}(V)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V) \xrightarrow{\ F_{\mathrm{CWX}}^V\ }\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_V).$$ The tensor structure and braiding on the right are Connes fusion and DHR braiding, with a common orientation convention for the circle.
  - Explanation: For every simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra, the smeared vertex operators give an irreducible conformal net. All simple modules are unitarizable with positive fusion forms, every unitary module is strongly integrable, the net is completely rational, and the Carpi-Weiner-Xu functor is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto the finite-index sectors.
- **claim-optimal-energy-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-energy)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra, and let $W$ be a grading-restricted unitary $V$-module. Every vertex operator on $W$ satisfies polynomial energy bounds. More precisely, if $a\in V_d$ is quasi-primary, $d\geq2$, and $(-1)^d\Theta a=a$, then there are constants $C_a>0$ and $p_a\in\mathbb Z_{\geq0}$ such that $$\label{eq:optimal-vertex-bound} \\|a[m]\xi\\| \leq C_a(1+\|m\|)^{p_a} \\|(1+L_0)^{d-1}\xi\\|, \qquad m\in\mathbb Z,\quad \xi\in W.$$ Weight-one fields and the Virasoro field satisfy energy bounds of at most linear order. The constants may depend on $V$, $W$, and $a$.
  - Explanation: Every vertex operator on a grading-restricted unitary module has polynomial energy bounds. A Hermitian quasi-primary field of weight at least two has a mode bound whose energy order is the weight minus one, which is the order the strong-locality criterion needs.
- **claim-energy-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-energy-filtration)): Every homogeneous vector of weight $w$ is a linear combination of monomials $$\label{eq:energy-monomial} b=a^1_{(-1-n_1)}\cdots a^\ell_{(-1-n_\ell)}\Omega, \qquad a^i\in S,\quad n_i\geq0,$$ with $$D_b:=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}d_{a^i},\qquad w=D_b+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_i.$$ If the vector belongs to $C_2(V)_w$, the monomials can be chosen with $D_b\leq w-1$.
  - Explanation: Every homogeneous vector is a combination of negative-mode monomials in a finite generating set of quasi-primary vectors. Vectors in the $C_2$ subspace admit monomials whose total generator weight is strictly below the conformal weight, which supplies the degree saving used in the contractions.
- **claim-sharp-normal-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sharp-normal-product)): For a monomial $b$ in <a href="#eq:energy-monomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-monomial">[eq:energy-monomial]</a> of weight $w\geq2$, there are constants $C_b,c_b$ such that $$\label{eq:sharp-normal-product} \\|b[0]P_N\\| \leq C_b N^{w-1}X_{N+c_b}^{D_b}\qquad(N\geq1).$$ At weights zero and one the corresponding bounds are a constant and $C_bN^{1/2}X_N$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The zero mode of a monomial of weight at least two satisfies a cutoff bound with a power of the energy cutoff equal to the weight minus one, times a power of the control quantity set by the generator weight. Counting the mode constraint saves one full power compared to the unconstrained count.
- **claim-strong-locality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-locality)): Every simple unitary strongly rational VOA $V$ is CKLW-strongly local. Its smeared vertex operators generate an irreducible conformal net $A_V$.
  - Explanation: Combining the optimal energy bounds with the corrected strong-locality criterion for Hermitian primary generators shows that the algebra is CKLW-strongly local, so its smeared fields generate an irreducible conformal net.
- **claim-annulus-range** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annulus-range)): Let $\underline A,\underline B$ be lifted annuli satisfying $\underline A\underline B=s_0^{L_0}$, with $0<s_0<1$, in a positive-energy representation with dense finite-energy subspace $E$. Then $$E\subset\operatorname{ran}\underline A, \qquad \underline A^{-1}E=\underline B E, \qquad \overline{\underline A^{-1}E}=\mathcal H.$$ Every radial annulus above admits such a complementary factor $\underline B$ when $s_0<\min\rho$.
  - Explanation: If a lifted annulus has a complementary lifted factor whose product is a dilation, the finite-energy subspace lies in the range of the annulus operator, the preimage equals the image of the complementary factor, and that preimage is dense.
- **claim-fixed-disc-insertion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-disc)): Let $\widehat A=\underline A^\dagger\underline A$ be a fixed localized double, and let $S_0$ be a closed round disc strictly inside it. There is a constant $C_{\widehat A,S_0}$ such that, for every Möbius embedding $p:\mathbb D\to\mathring S_0$ and $v\in V$, $$\label{eq:fixed-disc-bound} \\|\widehat A_N[p_*v(0)]\\| \leq C_{\widehat A,S_0}\\|v\\|$$ in every already integrated unitary module $N$. Here $\mathbb D$ is the closed unit disc, and embeddings are smooth up to their boundaries.
  - Explanation: Insertions of a state through a Möbius embedding into a fixed disc strictly inside a localized double annulus are bounded by a constant times the norm of the state, in every already integrated unitary module.
- **claim-uniform-round-annulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-round-annulus)): Let $N$ be an already integrated unitary $V$-module. Set $$a=1-10^{-4},\qquad \gamma_t(\zeta)=\frac{\zeta+t}{1+t\zeta},\qquad r=\gamma_t(a),\qquad s=\gamma_t(1/2),\qquad 0\leq t<1.$$ With $\delta=r^{-1}-r$ and $q=2\delta$, there is a constant $C_N$, independent of $t$ and $v$, such that $$\label{eq:uniform-round} \\|A_{1/s,s,N}[(q^{L_0}v)(r)]\\|\leq C_N\\|v\\|, \qquad v\in V.$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:round-comparable} 5000\leq\frac{1-s}{1-r}\leq6667.$$
  - Explanation: For round annuli degenerating towards an insertion point, the insertion operators are bounded uniformly in the Möbius parameter by a constant times the state norm, and the ratio of the boundary distances stays in a fixed interval.
- **claim-transport-cutoff** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport-cutoff)): Let $M,N$ be unitary modules, with $N$ already integrated. For homogeneous $a,a'\in M$ of weights $d,d'$, set $$u_z=\mathcal Y_M^-(\theta_{\bar z}a',\bar z^{-1}-z)a, \qquad \delta=\|z\|^{-1}-\|z\|,\qquad q=2\delta.$$ As $\|z\|$ tends to one on a fixed branch, $$\label{eq:annulus-hilbert-state} q^{-L_0}u_z\in\mathcal H_V, \qquad \\|q^{-L_0}u_z\\|\leq C_{a,a'}\delta^{-d-d'}.$$ For a fixed dent with $z$ in its inner collar, $\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]$ is well defined by norm limits of energy truncations, belongs to $\pi_I^N(A_V(I))$, and has norm at most $C\delta^{-d-d'}$. On $\underline A_N^{-1}N$ in both variables it obeys $$\label{eq:transport-cutoff} \langle\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]\xi,\eta\rangle =\mathfrak t_z^{M,N} (a\otimes\underline A_N\xi, a'\otimes\underline A_N\eta).$$ For the round annuli of Proposition <a href="#prop:round-annulus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:round-annulus">8</a>, the same bound holds with the cutoffs $s^{L_0}$ in both entries and $z=r$.
  - Explanation: Rescaled annihilation insertion states lie in the vacuum Hilbert space with a power-law bound in the distance to the circle. The corresponding annulus insertion is a bounded element of the local algebra that reproduces the transport form on exact dense preimages.
- **claim-charged-tools** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-charged-tools)): Let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ already strongly integrable, and let $\Phi$ have type $\binom{K}{M\,N}$. Fix a homogeneous $a\in M$ of weight $d$. Suppose that, for all finite-energy $b\in N$ and all diagonal positions sufficiently close to the unit circle on the branches under consideration, $$\label{eq:transport-domination} \\|\Phi(a,z)b\\|^2 \leq C_a\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}(a\otimes b,a\otimes b).$$ Then $\Phi(a,z)$ has polynomial energy bounds, as do its levelwise adjoint coefficients. Its smooth smears and their formal adjoints preserve the smooth domains continuously.* *For every smooth test $f$ supported compactly in an interval $I$ with a chosen argument, there is a localized radial annulus $\underline A$ with a dent over $\operatorname{supp}f$ such that $$\label{eq:charged-bounded-cutoff} T_f=\overline{\Phi(a,f)}\,\underline A_N :\mathcal H_N\longrightarrow\mathcal H_K$$ is bounded and defined on all of $\mathcal H_N$. Both $T_f$ and $T_f^*$ preserve smooth vectors in their respective directions. Moreover, $$N\subset\operatorname{ran}\underline A_N, \qquad \underline A_N^{-1}N\text{ is dense in }\mathcal H_N.$$ These conclusions apply to $\Phi=\mathcal Y_M^+$ and $N=V$ for every already unitary $M$, before integrating $M$.
  - Explanation: If an intertwiner is dominated by the transport form, it has polynomial energy bounds and smooth smears. Composed with a localized dented annulus, its closed smear is a bounded operator on the whole Hilbert space, which covers the creation case.
- **claim-strong-intertwining** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strong-intertwining)): Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, and let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ strongly integrable. Let $\Phi$ be an intertwining operator of type $\binom{K}{M\,N}$, and let $a\in M$ be homogeneous. Assume that $\Phi(a,z)$ satisfies the transport domination hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>. Smear $\Phi(a,z)$ against a smooth test $f$ supported in an interval $I$, with a fixed argument branch. If $J$ is disjoint from $I$, then the closed smeared operator $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$ strongly intertwines the diagonal smeared observables of $N$ and $K$ supported in $J$. This holds for every homogeneous observable in $V$ and for every such choice of tests and argument branches.
  - Explanation: A dominated intertwiner smeared on an interval strongly intertwines the diagonal observables of source and target supported in a disjoint interval, as closed operators and not only on a common dense domain.
- **claim-creation-totality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-creation-totality)): Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, let $M$ be a grading-restricted unitary $V$-module, and let $I$ be a nonempty interval. For every homogeneous $a\in M$ and $f\in C_c^\infty(I)$, form the bounded creation regularization $$T_{a,f}=\overline{\mathcal Y_M^+(a,f)}\underline A_V$$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>; the localized annulus may depend on the test. The closed linear span of their ranges is $\mathcal H_M$.
  - Explanation: Bounded creation regularizations applied to the vacuum have a total range in the module Hilbert space, proved by showing that a one-sided distribution vanishing on an arc is zero.
- **claim-strong-integration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-integration)): Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational. Every grading-restricted unitary $V$-module $M$ has a unique compatible family of normal representations $$\pi_J^M:A_V(J)\longrightarrow B(\mathcal H_M)$$ such that, for every homogeneous $v\in V$ and $g\in C_c^\infty(J)$, $$\pi_J^M\!\left(\overline{Y^V(v,g)}\right) =\overline{Y^M(v,g)}. \label{eq:exact-cwx-smear}$$ Equality here includes the domains of the closed affiliated operators. The canonical Virasoro action of this net representation is the integrated module Virasoro action; in particular its rotations are $e^{itL_0}$ with the module’s conformal-energy normalization.
  - Explanation: Every grading-restricted unitary module carries a unique compatible family of normal local representations of the vacuum net that map the closed vacuum smears to the closed module smears, with rotations given by the module energy operator.
- **claim-positive-fusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-positive-fusion)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. For any unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules $M,N$, let $X=M\boxtimes N$ and let $\mathcal Y_X$ be its universal intertwining operator. There is a positive-definite invariant Hermitian form $h_X$ on $X$ such that $$\mathfrak t^{M,N}_{z,w}(a\otimes b,a'\otimes b') =\sum_\lambda h_X\big( \Pi_\lambda\mathcal Y_X(a,z)b, \Pi_\lambda\mathcal Y_X(a',w)b'\big) \label{eq:positive-fusion-identity}$$ for all the vectors and coordinates in <a href="#eq:two-point-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-point-transport">[eq:two-point-transport]</a>–<a href="#eq:positive-transport-region" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-transport-region">[eq:positive-transport-region]</a>. The sum converges absolutely. This is the canonical positive normalization of the fusion form.
  - Explanation: The algebraic fusion product of two unitary finite-length modules carries a positive-definite invariant Hermitian form that expresses the two-point transport form as an absolutely convergent sum over conformal-energy projections, in the canonical normalization.
- **claim-positive-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-moments)): Let $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R$ be countable and bounded below. Suppose complex numbers $c_\lambda$ satisfy $$\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}e^{\epsilon\lambda}\|c_\lambda\|<\infty \quad\text{for some }\epsilon>0.$$ If $\sum_\lambda \|p(\lambda)\|^2c_\lambda$ is real and nonnegative for every complex polynomial $p$, then every $c_\lambda$ is real and nonnegative.
  - Explanation: If a countable family of complex weights has an exponential moment and all polynomial-weighted sums are real and nonnegative, then every individual weight is real and nonnegative.
- **claim-charged-general** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-charged-general)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA, and let $M,N,K$ be unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules. Every intertwining operator $\Phi$ of type $\binom{K}{M\ N}$ is polynomially energy-bounded for every homogeneous charge vector and has the strong intertwining property with all observable fields. More precisely, for homogeneous $a\in M$ of weight $d$, write $$\Phi(a,z)=\sum_\mu\Phi(a)[\mu]z^{-\mu-d}.$$ There are $C>0$ and nonnegative integers $s,k$ such that $$\\|\Phi(a)[\mu]b\\| \leq C(1+\|\mu\|)^s\\|(1+L_0)^k b\\| \qquad(b\in N).$$ If a smooth test $f$ is supported in a lifted interval $I$, the closed off-diagonal smear $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$ on $\mathcal H_N\oplus\mathcal H_K$ is affiliated with the commutant of the algebra generated by $$\operatorname{diag}\big(\overline{Y^N(v,g)}, \overline{Y^K(v,g)}\big), \qquad v\in V,\quad \operatorname{supp}g\subset J,$$ for every interval $J$ disjoint from $I$.
  - Explanation: Every intertwining operator between unitary finite-length modules is polynomially energy-bounded for each homogeneous charge vector and strongly intertwines the observable fields localized on disjoint intervals.
- **claim-strong-unitarity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-strong-unitarity)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra. Every simple grading-restricted $V$-module admits a positive definite invariant Hermitian form. Consequently, every grading-restricted finite-length $V$-module is unitarizable, and $V$ is strongly unitary.
  - Explanation: Every simple grading-restricted module admits a positive definite invariant Hermitian form, so every finite-length module is unitarizable and the algebra is strongly unitary. The proof uses the transposition-twisted module on the tensor square and a second form from the sign constituent.
- **claim-permutation-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity)): Let $V$ be simple and strongly rational, let $D=V\otimes V$, and let $\sigma$ exchange the factors. Write $T_\sigma(N)$ for the Barron–Dong–Mason transposition-twisted module associated to an ordinary $V$-module $N$ [[BDM](#ref-BDM), Theorem 3.9]. The fusion products below are taken among grading-restricted ordinary or $\sigma$-twisted $D$-modules. For every ordinary $V$-module $P$, $$(P\otimes P^\vee)\boxtimes_D T_\sigma(V) \cong T_\sigma(P\boxtimes_V P^\vee).$$ No unitarity or positivity of the lowest conformal weight of $P$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: Before any positivity is known, the fusion of a tensor-product module with the transposition-twisted vacuum module is the twisted module of the fusion of the two factors.
- **claim-cwx-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cwx-embedding)): Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational. The Hilbert completion and the local actions of Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a> define a fully faithful $*$-functor $$F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}: \operatorname{Rep}^u(V)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Rep}(A_V).$$ It has a unitary tensor structure identifying the Huang–Lepowsky fusion and braiding with Connes fusion and DHR braiding, respectively. In particular every object in its image is dualizable, hence has finite index.
  - Explanation: Hilbert completion with the integrated local actions gives a fully faithful star-functor with a unitary tensor structure matching Huang-Lepowsky fusion and braiding with Connes fusion and DHR braiding. Objects in its image are dualizable and of finite index.
- **claim-exhaustion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exhaustion)): Let $V\ne\mathbb C\Omega$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA which is energy-bounded and strongly local. Every nonzero irreducible locally normal representation $(\mathcal K,\pi)$ of $A_V$ is the strong integration of an ordinary simple unitary $V$-module. No finite-index or finite-energy-multiplicity assumption on $\pi$ is required.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero irreducible locally normal representation of the net is the strong integration of an ordinary simple unitary module, with no finite-index or finite-multiplicity assumption.
- **claim-complete-rationality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rationality)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. Its conformal net $A_V$ is completely rational. The CWX functor constructed in Theorem <a href="#thm:cwx-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cwx-embedding">19</a> is essentially surjective onto the additive category of finite-index locally normal DHR representations of $A_V$. Consequently it is a braided unitary tensor equivalence $$F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}: \operatorname{Rep}^{u}(V)\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ } \operatorname{Rep}^{f}(A_V).$$
  - Explanation: The net is completely rational and the integration functor is essentially surjective onto finite-index representations, hence a braided unitary tensor equivalence.
- **claim-extension-correspondence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions)): Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. Up to isomorphism over the embedded base, $U\mapsto A_U$ gives a one-to-one correspondence between the following classes:* 1. *normalized CFT-type unitary VOA extensions $V\hookrightarrow U$ whose underlying $V$-module is grading-restricted and of finite length;* 2. *normalized irreducible local conformal-net extensions of $A_V$ of finite index.* *The VOA class may equivalently be described by simple CFT-type algebraic extensions with the same conformal vector and finite-length underlying $V$-module, equipped with their unique unitary structure extending that of $V$.* *Let $P$ be the normalized haploid commutative $C^*$-Frobenius algebra of $U$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V)$, and let $\Theta$ be its transport to $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_V)$ under the base equivalence. The Q-system extension $B_\Theta$ equals $A_U$ as a conformal net on $\mathcal H_U$. Moreover, $A_U$ is completely rational and $$F^U_{\mathrm{CWX}}\circ F_{\mathrm{VOA}} =F_{\mathrm{CN}}\circ\widetilde F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}$$ as braided unitary tensor functors with Gui’s tensorators. Here $\widetilde F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}$ transports unitary local (dyslectic) $P$-modules to local $\Theta$-modules, and $F_{\mathrm{VOA}}$ and $F_{\mathrm{CN}}$ are the canonical equivalences from these categories to $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(U)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_U)$, respectively. In particular, $F^U_{\mathrm{CWX}}$ is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto all finite-index sectors of $A_U$.
  - Explanation: Finite-index normalized local net extensions correspond one to one with normalized unitary CFT-type VOA extensions of finite length, through Frobenius algebra objects. The extended net is completely rational and the extended integration functor is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto all its finite-index sectors.

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\underline A,\\underline B$ be lifted annuli satisfying $\\underline A\\underline B=s_0^{L_0}$, with $0<s_0<1$, in a positive-energy representation with dense finite-energy subspace $E$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-disc",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\widehat A=\\underline A^\\dagger\\underline A$ be a fixed localized double, and let $S_0$ be a closed round disc strictly inside it.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-round-annulus",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be an already integrated unitary $V$-module. Set $$a=1-10^{-4},\\qquad\n \\gamma_t(\\zeta)=\\frac{\\zeta+t}{1+t\\zeta},\\qquad\n r=\\gamma_t(a),\\qquad s=\\gamma_t(1/2),\\qquad 0\\leq t<1.$$ With $\\delta=r^{-1}-r$ and $q=2\\delta$, there is a constant $C_N$, independent of $t$ and $v$, such that $$\\label{eq:uniform-round}\n \\|A_{1/s,s,N}[(q^{L_0}v)(r)]\\|\\leq C_N\\|v\\|,\n \\qquad v\\in V.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:round-comparable}\n 5000\\leq\\frac{1-s}{1-r}\\leq6667.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-round-annulus",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be an already integrated unitary $V$-module.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport-cutoff",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M,N$ be unitary modules, with $N$ already integrated. For homogeneous $a,a'\\in M$ of weights $d,d'$, set $$u_z=\\mathcal Y_M^-(\\theta_{\\bar z}a',\\bar z^{-1}-z)a,\n \\qquad \\delta=|z|^{-1}-|z|,\\qquad q=2\\delta.$$ As $|z|$ tends to one on a fixed branch, $$\\label{eq:annulus-hilbert-state}\n q^{-L_0}u_z\\in\\mathcal H_V,\n \\qquad \\|q^{-L_0}u_z\\|\\leq C_{a,a'}\\delta^{-d-d'}.$$ For a fixed dent with $z$ in its inner collar, $\\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]$ is well defined by norm limits of energy truncations, belongs to $\\pi_I^N(A_V(I))$, and has norm at most $C\\delta^{-d-d'}$. On $\\underline A_N^{-1}N$ in both variables it obeys $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoff}\n \\langle\\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]\\xi,\\eta\\rangle\n =\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}\n   (a\\otimes\\underline A_N\\xi,\n    a'\\otimes\\underline A_N\\eta).$$ For the round annuli of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:round-annulus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:round-annulus\">8</a>, the same bound holds with the cutoffs $s^{L_0}$ in both entries and $z=r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport-cutoff",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M,N$ be unitary modules, with $N$ already integrated. On $\\underline A_N^{-1}N$ in both variables it obeys $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoff}\n \\langle\\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]\\xi,\\eta\\rangle\n =\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}\n   (a\\otimes\\underline A_N\\xi,\n    a'\\otimes\\underline A_N\\eta).$$ For the round annuli of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:round-annulus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:round-annulus\">8</a>, the same bound holds with the cutoffs $s^{L_0}$ in both entries and $z=r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-280.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $M,N$ be unitary modules, with $N$ already integrated. On $\\underline A_N^{-1}N$ in both variables it obeys $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoff}\n \\langle\\widehat A_N[u_z(z)]\\xi,\\eta\\rangle\n =\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}\n   (a\\otimes\\underline A_N\\xi,\n    a'\\otimes\\underline A_N\\eta).$$ For the round annuli of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:round-annulus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:round-annulus\">8</a>, the same bound holds with the cutoffs $s^{L_0}$ in both entries and $z=r$.",
      "explanation": "Rescaled annihilation insertion states lie in the vacuum Hilbert space with a power-law bound in the distance to the circle. The corresponding annulus insertion is a bounded element of the local algebra that reproduces the transport form on exact dense preimages.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-charged-tools",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ already strongly integrable, and let $\\Phi$ have type $\\binom{K}{M\\,N}$. Fix a homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$. Suppose that, for all finite-energy $b\\in N$ and all diagonal positions sufficiently close to the unit circle on the branches under consideration, $$\\label{eq:transport-domination}\n \\|\\Phi(a,z)b\\|^2\n   \\leq C_a\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}(a\\otimes b,a\\otimes b).$$ Then $\\Phi(a,z)$ has polynomial energy bounds, as do its levelwise adjoint coefficients. Its smooth smears and their formal adjoints preserve the smooth domains continuously.*\n\n\n*For every smooth test $f$ supported compactly in an interval $I$ with a chosen argument, there is a localized radial annulus $\\underline A$ with a dent over $\\operatorname{supp}f$ such that $$\\label{eq:charged-bounded-cutoff}\n T_f=\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}\\,\\underline A_N\n       :\\mathcal H_N\\longrightarrow\\mathcal H_K$$ is bounded and defined on all of $\\mathcal H_N$. Both $T_f$ and $T_f^*$ preserve smooth vectors in their respective directions. Moreover, $$N\\subset\\operatorname{ran}\\underline A_N,\n \\qquad \\underline A_N^{-1}N\\text{ is dense in }\\mathcal H_N.$$ These conclusions apply to $\\Phi=\\mathcal Y_M^+$ and $N=V$ for every already unitary $M$, before integrating $M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-charged-tools",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ already strongly integrable, and let $\\Phi$ have type $\\binom{K}{M\\,N}$. Fix a homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$. Suppose that, for all finite-energy $b\\in N$ and all diagonal positions sufficiently close to the unit circle on the branches under consideration, $$\\label{eq:transport-domination}\n \\|\\Phi(a,z)b\\|^2\n   \\leq C_a\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}(a\\otimes b,a\\otimes b).$$ Then $\\Phi(a,z)$ has polynomial energy bounds, as do its levelwise adjoint coefficients. Its smooth smears and their formal adjoints preserve the smooth domains continuously.*\n\n\n*For every smooth test $f$ supported compactly in an interval $I$ with a chosen argument, there is a localized radial annulus $\\underline A$ with a dent over $\\operatorname{supp}f$ such that $$\\label{eq:charged-bounded-cutoff}\n T_f=\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}\\,\\underline A_N\n       :\\mathcal H_N\\longrightarrow\\mathcal H_K$$ is bounded and defined on all of $\\mathcal H_N$. Both $T_f$ and $T_f^*$ preserve smooth vectors in their respective directions. Moreover, $$N\\subset\\operatorname{ran}\\underline A_N,\n \\qquad \\underline A_N^{-1}N\\text{ is dense in }\\mathcal H_N.$$ These conclusions apply to $\\Phi=\\mathcal Y_M^+$ and $N=V$ for every already unitary $M$, before integrating $M$.",
          "source_id": "PM-280.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ already strongly integrable, and let $\\Phi$ have type $\\binom{K}{M\\,N}$. Fix a homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$. Suppose that, for all finite-energy $b\\in N$ and all diagonal positions sufficiently close to the unit circle on the branches under consideration, $$\\label{eq:transport-domination}\n \\|\\Phi(a,z)b\\|^2\n   \\leq C_a\\mathfrak t_z^{M,N}(a\\otimes b,a\\otimes b).$$ Then $\\Phi(a,z)$ has polynomial energy bounds, as do its levelwise adjoint coefficients. Its smooth smears and their formal adjoints preserve the smooth domains continuously.*\n\n\n*For every smooth test $f$ supported compactly in an interval $I$ with a chosen argument, there is a localized radial annulus $\\underline A$ with a dent over $\\operatorname{supp}f$ such that $$\\label{eq:charged-bounded-cutoff}\n T_f=\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}\\,\\underline A_N\n       :\\mathcal H_N\\longrightarrow\\mathcal H_K$$ is bounded and defined on all of $\\mathcal H_N$. Both $T_f$ and $T_f^*$ preserve smooth vectors in their respective directions. Moreover, $$N\\subset\\operatorname{ran}\\underline A_N,\n \\qquad \\underline A_N^{-1}N\\text{ is dense in }\\mathcal H_N.$$ These conclusions apply to $\\Phi=\\mathcal Y_M^+$ and $N=V$ for every already unitary $M$, before integrating $M$.",
      "explanation": "If an intertwiner is dominated by the transport form, it has polynomial energy bounds and smooth smears. Composed with a localized dented annulus, its closed smear is a bounded operator on the whole Hilbert space, which covers the creation case.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "source_id": "PM-280.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-strong-intertwining",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, and let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ strongly integrable. Let $\\Phi$ be an intertwining operator of type $\\binom{K}{M\\,N}$, and let $a\\in M$ be homogeneous. Assume that $\\Phi(a,z)$ satisfies the transport domination hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:charged-tools\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:charged-tools\">10</a>. Smear $\\Phi(a,z)$ against a smooth test $f$ supported in an interval $I$, with a fixed argument branch. If $J$ is disjoint from $I$, then the closed smeared operator $\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}$ strongly intertwines the diagonal smeared observables of $N$ and $K$ supported in $J$. This holds for every homogeneous observable in $V$ and for every such choice of tests and argument branches.",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-strong-intertwining",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, and let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ strongly integrable. Assume that $\\Phi(a,z)$ satisfies the transport domination hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:charged-tools\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:charged-tools\">10</a>. Smear $\\Phi(a,z)$ against a smooth test $f$ supported in an interval $I$, with a fixed argument branch. If $J$ is disjoint from $I$, then the closed smeared operator $\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}$ strongly intertwines the diagonal smeared observables of $N$ and $K$ supported in $J$. This holds for every homogeneous observable in $V$ and for every such choice of tests and argument branches.",
          "source_id": "PM-280.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, and let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ strongly integrable. This holds for every homogeneous observable in $V$ and for every such choice of tests and argument branches.",
      "explanation": "A dominated intertwiner smeared on an interval strongly intertwines the diagonal observables of source and target supported in a disjoint interval, as closed operators and not only on a common dense domain.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, let $M$ be a grading-restricted unitary $V$-module, and let $I$ be a nonempty interval. For every homogeneous $a\\in M$ and $f\\in C_c^\\infty(I)$, form the bounded creation regularization $$T_{a,f}=\\overline{\\mathcal Y_M^+(a,f)}\\underline A_V$$ of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:charged-tools\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:charged-tools\">10</a>; the localized annulus may depend on the test.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-integration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational. Every grading-restricted unitary $V$-module $M$ has a unique compatible family of normal representations $$\\pi_J^M:A_V(J)\\longrightarrow B(\\mathcal H_M)$$ such that, for every homogeneous $v\\in V$ and $g\\in C_c^\\infty(J)$, $$\\pi_J^M\\!\\left(\\overline{Y^V(v,g)}\\right)\n       =\\overline{Y^M(v,g)}.\n \\label{eq:exact-cwx-smear}$$ Equality here includes the domains of the closed affiliated operators. The canonical Virasoro action of this net representation is the integrated module Virasoro action; in particular its rotations are $e^{itL_0}$ with the module’s conformal-energy normalization.",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-integration",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-positive-fusion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. For any unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules $M,N$, let $X=M\\boxtimes N$ and let $\\mathcal Y_X$ be its universal intertwining operator. There is a positive-definite invariant Hermitian form $h_X$ on $X$ such that $$\\mathfrak t^{M,N}_{z,w}(a\\otimes b,a'\\otimes b')\n =\\sum_\\lambda h_X\\big(\n \\Pi_\\lambda\\mathcal Y_X(a,z)b,\n \\Pi_\\lambda\\mathcal Y_X(a',w)b'\\big)\n \\label{eq:positive-fusion-identity}$$ for all the vectors and coordinates in <a href=\"#eq:two-point-transport\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:two-point-transport\">[eq:two-point-transport]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:positive-transport-region\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:positive-transport-region\">[eq:positive-transport-region]</a>.         "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-charged-general",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA, and let $M,N,K$ be unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules. Every intertwining operator $\\Phi$ of type $\\binom{K}{M\\ N}$ is polynomially energy-bounded for every homogeneous charge vector and has the strong intertwining property with all observable fields. More precisely, for homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$, write $$\\Phi(a,z)=\\sum_\\mu\\Phi(a)[\\mu]z^{-\\mu-d}.$$ There are $C>0$ and nonnegative integers $s,k$ such that $$\\|\\Phi(a)[\\mu]b\\|\n \\leq C(1+|\\mu|)^s\\|(1+L_0)^k b\\|\n \\qquad(b\\in N).$$ If a smooth test $f$ is supported in a lifted interval $I$, the closed off-diagonal smear $\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}$ on $\\mathcal H_N\\oplus\\mathcal H_K$ is affiliated with the commutant of the algebra generated by $$\\operatorname{diag}\\big(\\overline{Y^N(v,g)},\n                         \\overline{Y^K(v,g)}\\big),\n \\qquad v\\in V,\\quad \\operatorname{supp}g\\subset J,$$ for every interval $J$ disjoint from $I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-280.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-charged-general",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA, and let $M,N,K$ be unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules. Every intertwining operator $\\Phi$ of type $\\binom{K}{M\\ N}$ is polynomially energy-bounded for every homogeneous charge vector and has the strong intertwining property with all observable fields. More precisely, for homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$, write $$\\Phi(a,z)=\\sum_\\mu\\Phi(a)[\\mu]z^{-\\mu-d}.$$ There are $C>0$ and nonnegative integers $s,k$ such that $$\\|\\Phi(a)[\\mu]b\\|\n \\leq C(1+|\\mu|)^s\\|(1+L_0)^k b\\|\n \\qquad(b\\in N).$$ If a smooth test $f$ is supported in a lifted interval $I$, the closed off-diagonal smear $\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}$ on $\\mathcal H_N\\oplus\\mathcal H_K$ is affiliated with the commutant of the algebra generated by $$\\operatorname{diag}\\big(\\overline{Y^N(v,g)},\n                         \\overline{Y^K(v,g)}\\big),\n \\qquad v\\in V,\\quad \\operatorname{supp}g\\subset J,$$ for every interval $J$ disjoint from $I$.",
          "source_id": "PM-280.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA, and let $M,N,K$ be unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules. Every intertwining operator $\\Phi$ of type $\\binom{K}{M\\ N}$ is polynomially energy-bounded for every homogeneous charge vector and has the strong intertwining property with all observable fields. More precisely, for homogeneous $a\\in M$ of weight $d$, write $$\\Phi(a,z)=\\sum_\\mu\\Phi(a)[\\mu]z^{-\\mu-d}.$$ There are $C>0$ and nonnegative integers $s,k$ such that $$\\|\\Phi(a)[\\mu]b\\|\n \\leq C(1+|\\mu|)^s\\|(1+L_0)^k b\\|\n \\qquad(b\\in N).$$ If a smooth test $f$ is supported in a lifted interval $I$, the closed off-diagonal smear $\\overline{\\Phi(a,f)}$ on $\\mathcal H_N\\oplus\\mathcal H_K$ is affiliated with the commutant of the algebra generated by $$\\operatorname{diag}\\big(\\overline{Y^N(v,g)},\n                         \\overline{Y^K(v,g)}\\big),\n \\qquad v\\in V,\\quad \\operatorname{supp}g\\subset J,$$ for every interval $J$ disjoint from $I$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be simple and strongly rational, let $D=V\\otimes V$, and let $\\sigma$ exchange the factors. The fusion products below are taken among grading-restricted ordinary or $\\sigma$-twisted $D$-modules. For every ordinary $V$-module $P$, $$(P\\otimes P^\\vee)\\boxtimes_D T_\\sigma(V)\n \\cong T_\\sigma(P\\boxtimes_V P^\\vee).$$ No unitarity or positivity of the lowest conformal weight of $P$ is assumed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. Up to isomorphism over the embedded base, $U\\mapsto A_U$ gives a one-to-one correspondence between the following classes:*\n\n1.  *normalized CFT-type unitary VOA extensions $V\\hookrightarrow U$ whose underlying $V$-module is grading-restricted and of finite length;*\n\n2.  *normalized irreducible local conformal-net extensions of $A_V$ of finite index.*\n\n*The VOA class may equivalently be described by simple CFT-type algebraic extensions with the same conformal vector and finite-length underlying $V$-module, equipped with their unique unitary structure extending that of $V$.*\n\n*Let $P$ be the normalized haploid commutative $C^*$-Frobenius algebra of $U$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V)$, and let $\\Theta$ be its transport to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_V)$ under the base equivalence. The Q-system extension $B_\\Theta$ equals $A_U$ as a conformal net on $\\mathcal H_U$. Moreover, $A_U$ is completely rational and $$F^U_{\\mathrm{CWX}}\\circ F_{\\mathrm{VOA}}\n   =F_{\\mathrm{CN}}\\circ\\widetilde F^V_{\\mathrm{CWX}}$$ as braided unitary tensor functors with Gui’s tensorators. Here $\\widetilde F^V_{\\mathrm{CWX}}$ transports unitary local (dyslectic) $P$-modules to local $\\Theta$-modules, and $F_{\\mathrm{VOA}}$ and $F_{\\mathrm{CN}}$ are the canonical equivalences from these categories to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rep}}^u(U)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_U)$, respectively. In particular, $F^U_{\\mathrm{CWX}}$ is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto all finite-index sectors of $A_U$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **CFT type** ([source](#pm-h-3)): It is of *CFT type* if $$V=\bigoplus_{n\geq0}V_n,\qquad V_0=\mathbb C\Omega.$$
- **strongly rational** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We call $V$ *strongly rational* if it is of CFT type, is isomorphic to its graded contragredient, is rational, and has finite-dimensional quotient $V/C_2(V)$.
- **unitarity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Unitarity means that $V$ has a normalized positive invariant Hermitian form and its corresponding antilinear PCT involution $\Theta$.
- **strongly unitary and completely unitary** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We call $V$ *strongly unitary* when every simple module is unitarizable, and *completely unitary* when, in addition, the canonical invariant Hermitian fusion forms are positive definite.
- **unitary module category** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The category $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V)$ consists of such modules with positive invariant Hermitian forms, and module homomorphisms.
- **completely rational conformal net** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A conformal net $A$ is *completely rational* if it is irreducible, split, strongly additive and has finite $\mu$-index.
- **split net** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Split means that separated interval algebras generate their spatial tensor product.
- **mu-index** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For two disjoint intervals $E=I_1\cup I_3$, the $\mu$-index is the index of $A(E)\subset A(E')'$.
- **Hermitian dual** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an arbitrary module its *Hermitian dual* is $$M^{\mathrm h}=(\bar M)^\vee.$$
- **quasi-primary and primary states** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A state is *quasi-primary* if $L_1a=0$, and *primary* if $L_na=0$ for every $n>0$.
- **strong intertwining of observables** ([source](#sec:integration)): We say that $T$ *strongly intertwines the observables in $J$* when $\widetilde T$ is affiliated with $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)'$.

| q-first-contraction-exponent | exponent of the first energy contraction | = | $1-\frac1{2D}$ | integer bound on weights where the $C_2$ subspace is not everything |
| q-second-contraction-exponent | exponent of the second energy contraction | = | $1-\frac1{2D}$ | same integer bound as the first contraction |
| q-optimal-energy-order | energy order of a Hermitian quasi-primary mode bound | = | $d-1$ | quasi-primary states of weight at least two with the Hermitian condition |
| q-virasoro-linear-bound | Virasoro mode norm bound | ≤ | $C(1+\|m\|)^2\\|H\xi\\|$ | all integer modes |
| q-mobius-tail-multiplier | integer multiplier of the spectral tail cutoff | > | $4C_g$ | independent of the cutoff and of level dimensions |
| q-round-ratio-lower | ratio of boundary distances | ≥ | $5000$ | all parameters with the stated range |
| q-round-ratio-upper | ratio of boundary distances | ≤ | $6667$ | all parameters with the stated range |
| q-base-radius | fixed base radius of the round annulus family | = | $1-10^{-4}$ | fixed |
| q-central-charge-norm | squared norm of the conformal vector | = | $c/2$ | central charge c |
| q-twisted-energy | conformal energy of the transposition-twisted vacuum module | = | $\tfrac12 L_0^V+\tfrac{c}{16}$ | central charge c of V |
| q-two-interval-index | two-interval index of the net | = | $\sum_{i=1}^s d(M_i)^2$ | simple modules with positive forms |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-finite-generation** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-filtration): induction on generator weight using iterate formula and C2 degree saving -> negative-mode monomial expansions
- **step-sharp-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-sharp-normal-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sharp-normal-product): left-right mode expansion and Cauchy-Schwarz over constrained index pairs -> zero-mode cutoff bound
- **step-energy-bounds** [claim](#stmt-thm-energy) / [proof](#pm-h-16): two contractions from an exponential start, Möbius transfer to the zero mode -> optimal polynomial energy bounds
- **step-strong-locality** [claim](#stmt-cor-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-locality): apply the strong-locality criterion to Hermitian primary generators -> irreducible vacuum net
- **step-annulus-range** [claim](#stmt-lem-annulus-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annulus-range): factor a dilation through complementary annuli -> dense exact preimages
- **step-fixed-disc** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-disc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-disc): multidisc embedding bound and contractive disc insertion -> uniform insertion norm bound
- **step-round-annulus** [claim](#stmt-prop-round-annulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-round-annulus): factor the shrinking round annulus through one fixed dented annulus -> uniform round-annulus bound
- **step-transport-cutoff** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport-cutoff): weight scaling of annihilation values and norm-convergent truncations -> bounded transport insertions
- **step-charged-tools** [claim](#stmt-lem-charged-tools) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-charged-tools): radial estimate, mode bounds and Taylor argument at the boundary -> bounded regularized charged smears
- **step-strong-intertwining** [claim](#stmt-prop-strong-intertwining) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-strong-intertwining): Jacobi identity on smooth cores and passage to graph projections -> affiliation with the commutant
- **step-creation-totality** [claim](#stmt-lem-creation-totality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-creation-totality): one-sided boundary distribution vanishing on an arc -> total creation ranges
- **step-integration** [claim](#stmt-thm-integration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-integration): vacuum restriction on the diagonal algebra is a normal isomorphism -> normal local representations on every unitary module
- **step-positive-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-moments): density of polynomials in the weighted space and approximation of atoms -> nonnegativity at each energy
- **step-positive-fusion** [claim](#stmt-thm-positive-fusion) / [proof](#pm-h-26): positive Gram matrix in the vacuum algebra, rigid invariant form, moment separation -> positive fusion form
- **step-charged-general** [claim](#stmt-cor-charged-general) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-charged-general): factor through the universal intertwiner and use domination -> bounds for all intertwiners
- **step-permutation-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity): relative fusion over the fixed-point algebra and one-slot intertwiner dictionary -> twisted fusion isomorphism
- **step-strong-unitarity** [claim](#stmt-thm-strong-unitarity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity-2): detection in a positive fusion product plus a second form from the sign constituent -> positive invariant form on every simple module
- **step-cwx-embedding** [claim](#stmt-thm-cwx-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-cwx-embedding): verify the hypotheses of the categorical comparison theorem -> braided unitary tensor embedding
- **step-exhaustion** [claim](#stmt-prop-exhaustion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exhaustion): reconstruct a weak module, apply regularity and a reducing projection -> every sector is an integrated simple module
- **step-complete-rationality** [claim](#stmt-thm-rationality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-rationality): finite sector list, split property and the dichotomy -> complete rationality and equivalence
- **step-main-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-5): assemble the five assertions from the preceding results -> correspondence theorem
- **step-extensions** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions): transport Frobenius algebras across the base equivalence -> extension correspondence

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-net-from-vertex-algebra**: For a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra over the complex numbers, the smeared vertex operators generate an irreducible conformal net and the net is CKLW-strongly local.
- **inv-energy-bound-order**: On a grading-restricted unitary module, a Hermitian quasi-primary field of weight d at least two obeys a polynomial energy bound whose Hamiltonian order is d minus one.
- **inv-complete-rationality-equivalence**: The net is completely rational and the Carpi-Weiner-Xu functor is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto the finite-index sectors of the net.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-unitary-theory**: Apply the net construction and the module equivalence to a vertex operator algebra that has no positive invariant Hermitian form. -> Without positivity the closable smeared fields and Hilbert completions are unavailable, so no local von Neumann algebras are produced.
- **anti-non-strongly-rational**: Use the results for a vertex operator algebra that is irrational or lacks finite-dimensional quotient by the C2 subspace. -> The finite generating filtration, finiteness of simple modules and rigidity inputs are not available, so the argument breaks down.
- **anti-executable-solver**: Treat the equivalence as a computational procedure that produces fusion data or nets from an input algebra. -> The proofs are analytic existence arguments with no finite algorithm, so no output can be certified by running code.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are analytic existence and equivalence theorems about operator algebras and tensor categories: they rely on energy estimates, closed unbounded operators, von Neumann algebras and reconstruction arguments, and state no finite procedure whose output could be computed and checked.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence, boundedness and equivalence statements by analytic and categorical arguments. It gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that a program could execute, so there is nothing to implement as a library or service. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The setting is chiral conformal field theory on the circle, expressed through vertex operator algebras and nets of von Neumann algebras. There is no dynamical system or discretised model here that a simulation could reproduce. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results are structural theorems about a class of algebras. They supply no checker, converter or solver, and the hypotheses (simple, unitary, strongly rational) are not decidable by a generic tool from the paper alone. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proofs use contraction inequalities, positivity arguments and reconstruction theorems. These are existential and asymptotic, and none is a procedure with inputs, outputs and a cost that could be stated as an algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The net assigns von Neumann algebras of operators on infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces to intervals. These are not finitely representable objects, so no concrete data layout follows from the statements. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic kernels, parallelism or memory behaviour. The objects are unbounded operators and categories, with no computational workload to accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary model or security parameter appears. The paper is purely about mathematical physics structures, so no cryptographic primitive can be derived from it. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, loss, learning objective or statistical estimation. Positivity and unitarity here are algebraic properties of Hermitian forms, not learned quantities. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective function, constraint set or scheduling problem is formulated. The sharp energy order arises from a contraction argument, not from an optimisation problem to be solved. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages, failures or consensus questions. Locality in the paper means commutation of observables on disjoint circle intervals, not locality of communication. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural downstream context is foundational mathematical physics, for example comparing algebraic and operator-algebraic chiral theories and classifying finite-index extensions. This is conceptual transfer rather than an engineering construction, so no domain here qualifies as applicable. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-unitarity-hypothesis** (hypothesis): Every result assumes that the algebra carries a normalized positive invariant Hermitian form with its antilinear PCT involution. Without it the smeared fields are not known to give closable operators on Hilbert completions, so no local von Neumann algebras arise. Blocked use: ['Applying the net construction to a non-unitary or logarithmic vertex operator algebra', 'Using the module equivalence when no positive invariant form is available']
- **limit-strong-rationality** (hypothesis): Strong rationality (CFT type, self-contragredience, rationality, finite-dimensional quotient by the C2 subspace) is the regularity hypothesis behind the finite generating filtration, finiteness of the simple modules, rigidity and the sector count. The results give no information once any of these conditions fails. Blocked use: ['Using the correspondence for an irrational algebra', 'Using the correspondence for an algebra whose quotient by the C2 subspace is infinite-dimensional']
- **limit-energy-constants** (approximation): The energy bounds assert that constants exist and that they may depend on the algebra, the module and the field. No explicit values or uniformity over all fields or modules is given. Blocked use: ['Treating the constants in the energy bound as computable numbers', 'Claiming a uniform bound over all modules']
- **limit-monomial-uniformity** (edge_case): The monomial expansions and their constants are fixed finitely many at a time; the argument does not assert any uniformity over all monomials. Blocked use: ['Extrapolating a single constant to all monomials of arbitrary weight']
- **limit-scalar-case-split** (edge_case): The scalar algebra is treated separately; the analytic and reconstruction arguments assume the algebra is not the scalar one, and the nontriviality is used in the flip-orbifold argument and the sector theory. Blocked use: ['Reading the non-scalar proofs as covering the scalar algebra without the separate argument']
- **limit-integration-precedes-unitarity** (formal_scope): Strong integration is proved only for modules that already carry a positive form. Unitarizability of every simple module is a later and separate result. Blocked use: ['Applying the integration theorem to a simple module before a positive invariant form is known']
- **limit-permutation-algebraic-only** (formal_scope): The permutation product isomorphism is purely algebraic and gives no positivity or unitarity of the module on its own; positivity enters only through the later argument. Blocked use: ['Deducing a positive form on a simple module from the isomorphism alone']
- **limit-braiding-orientation** (hypothesis): The identification of the braidings holds with a common orientation convention for the circle, and after a choice of invariant positive forms on the simple modules. Blocked use: ['Comparing the two braidings under opposite orientation conventions']
- **limit-exhaustion-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The exhaustion of sectors is stated for a non-scalar algebra that is energy-bounded and strongly local, with the nonzero irreducible locally normal representation arbitrary but irreducible. Blocked use: ['Applying the sector reconstruction to a reducible representation without decomposing it first', 'Applying it to an algebra that is not energy-bounded and strongly local']
- **limit-nonconstructive-density** (nonconstructive): Density of polynomials in the weighted space is proved by an abstract orthogonality and uniqueness argument, so the positivity extraction yields no explicit procedure. Blocked use: ['Reading the moment lemma as an algorithm that recovers the energy-level weights']
- **limit-extension-normalization** (formal_scope): The extension correspondence holds only up to isomorphism over the embedded base and for normalized extensions of the stated classes, not for arbitrary extensions. Blocked use: ['Identifying non-normalized or infinite-index extensions with net extensions']
- **limit-optimal-order-locality** (approximation): The sharp energy order is what is needed for ordinary fields to generate a local net; for charged fields polynomial bounds with annulus regularization suffice, so the two orders are not interchangeable. Blocked use: ['Using a merely polynomial bound of unspecified order as input to the strong-locality criterion']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 280 (`lean/docs/280.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Strongly rational unitary vertex operator algebras and conformal nets](../../preprints/Strongly-rational-unitary-vertex-operator-algebras-and-conformal-nets-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization proves the first construction step relating strongly rational unitary vertex operator algebras to conformal nets. For a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra, it establishes polynomial energy bounds and strong locality and constructs an irreducible conformal net with the stated covariance, vacuum, and positive-energy properties.
>
> The selected statement does not include complete rationality of the net, unitarizability of all simple modules, the braided tensor equivalence, or the classification of local extensions described in the paper.

structure VertexAlgebra (V : Type u) [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V] where
  vacuum : V
  fields : OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.StateField V V
  vacuum_mode : ∀ (n : ℤ), OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields vacuum n = if n = -1 then 1 else 0
  creation : ∀ a : V, OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields a (-1) vacuum = a
  creation_truncation : ∀ (a : V) (n : ℤ), 0 ≤ n → OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields a n vacuum = 0
  jacobi : OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.JacobiIdentity fields

```lean
structure CFTTypeVOA (V : Type u) [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V]
    extends OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.VertexAlgebra V where
  conformalVector : V
  centralCharge : ℂ
  grade : ℕ → Submodule ℂ V
  grading_internal : DirectSum.IsInternal grade
  grade_finite : ∀ d, FiniteDimensional ℂ (grade d)
  vacuum_ne_zero : vacuum ≠ 0
  grade_zero : grade 0 = ℂ ∙ vacuum
  conformal_mem : conformalVector ∈ grade 2
  conformal_energy : ∀ d, ∀ a ∈ grade d,
    OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector 1 a = (d : ℂ) • a
  conformal_translation : OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector 0 = toVertexAlgebra.translation
  translation_covariance : ∀ a : V, ∀ n : ℤ,
    OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields (toVertexAlgebra.translation a) n =
      (-(n : ℂ)) • OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields a (n - 1)
  virasoro : ∀ m n : ℤ,
    (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector (m + 1)).comp (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector (n + 1)) -
      (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector (n + 1)).comp (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector (m + 1)) =
    ((m - n : ℤ) : ℂ) • OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode fields conformalVector (m + n + 1) +
      (if m + n = 0 then (centralCharge / 12) * ((m : ℂ)^3 - m) else 0) •
        (1 : Module.End ℂ V)
```

```lean
structure UnitaryStructure where
  pct : V →ₛₗ[starRingEnd ℂ] V
  pct_involutive : Function.Involutive pct
  pct_vacuum : pct A.vacuum = A.vacuum
  pct_conformal : pct A.conformalVector = A.conformalVector
  pct_modes : ∀ (a b : V) (n : ℤ),
    pct (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode A.fields a n b) = OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode A.fields (pct a) n (pct b)
  vacuum_norm : ‖A.vacuum‖ = 1
  invariant : ∀ d : ℕ, ∀ a ∈ A.grade d, ∀ n : ℤ, ∀ b c : V,
    inner ℂ (OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode A.fields a n b) c =
      inner ℂ b (((-1 : ℂ) ^ d) •
        ∑ j ∈ Finset.range (d + 1), ((j.factorial : ℂ)⁻¹) •
          OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.mode A.fields ((A.virasoroMode 1 ^ j) (pct a))
            (2 * (d : ℤ) - j - n - 2) c)
```

```lean
structure IrreducibleConformalNetStructure {H : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup H]
    [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]
    (N : OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc → VonNeumannAlgebra H) where
  separable : TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace H
  carrier_congr : ∀ I J, (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier I)=(OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier J) → N I=N J
  isotony : ∀ I J, (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier I) ⊆ (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier J) → N I ≤ N J
  locality : ∀ I J, Disjoint (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier I) (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier J) → N I ≤ (N J).commutant
  mobius : PSL(2,ℝ) →* (H ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] H)
  mobius_continuous : Continuous mobius
  diffeomorphism : OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.SmoothCircleDiffeomorphism →* OAI.StronglyRationalVOA.ProjectiveUnitaryGroup H
  diffeomorphism_continuous : Continuous diffeomorphism
  extends_mobius : ∀ F, diffeomorphism ((OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.CircleMobiusGeometry.inclusion) (OAI.MinimalVertex.projectiveStandardAction F))=QuotientGroup.mk (mobius F)
  covariance : ∀ F I J, (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier J)=(OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.SmoothCircleDiffeomorphism.action) F '' (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier I) →
    ∀ B : H →L[ℂ] H, OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.projectiveConjugation (diffeomorphism F) B ∈ N J ↔ B ∈ N I
  local_action : ∀ F I, (∀ x ∈ (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.ProperCircleArc.carrier I), (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.SmoothCircleDiffeomorphism.action) F x=x) →
    ∀ B ∈ N I, OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.projectiveConjugation (diffeomorphism F) B=B
  vacuum : H
  vacuum_norm : ‖vacuum‖=1
  vacuum_invariant : ∀ F, mobius F vacuum=vacuum
  vacuum_unique : ∀ x : H, (∀ F, mobius F x=x) → ∃ c : ℂ, x=c • vacuum
  vacuum_cyclic : (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.globalCyclicSpace N vacuum)=⊤
  hamiltonian : H →ₗ.[ℂ] H
  hamiltonian_selfAdjoint : IsSelfAdjoint hamiltonian
  positive_energy : ∀ x y, (x,y) ∈ hamiltonian.graph → 0 ≤ (inner ℂ x y).re
  rotation_flow : ∀ t : ℝ,
    (mobius (QuotientGroup.mk (OAI.MinimalVertex.Code.StronglyRationalVOA.CircleMobiusGeometry.rotationMatrix t))).toLinearIsometry.toContinuousLinearMap=
      OAI.MinimalVertex.flow hamiltonian hamiltonian_selfAdjoint t
  irreducible : ∀ Q : H →L[ℂ] H, (∀ I, Q ∈ (N I).commutant) →
    ∃ c : ℂ, Q=c • (1 : H →L[ℂ] H)
```

```lean
theorem main (_hSimple : A.toVertexAlgebra.IsSimple)
    (hSR : A.IsStronglyRational.{u,v}) :
    A.PolynomialEnergyBounds ∧ OAI.MinimalVertex.CKLWStrongLocal U hSR.2.2 ∧
      Nonempty (OAI.MinimalVertex.IrreducibleConformalNetStructure (OAI.MinimalVertex.intervalAlgebra U hSR.2.2))
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra generates a completely rational conformal net. All its simple grading-restricted modules are unitarizable, its canonical fusion forms are positive, and the Carpi–Weiner–Xu functor gives a braided unitary tensor equivalence from its grading-restricted finite-length module category onto the finite-index sectors of the net. Gui's extension theorems then identify normalized irreducible finite-index local extensions of these nets with simple CFT-type conformal extensions of the vertex operator algebras.
