This also shows that finite-energy truncations converge in the graph norm of the smear. The closures of the smears defined on finite-energy and on smooth vectors consequently agree.

<a id="eq:charged-collar"></a>
Choose $\underline A$ with $\rho<1$ on a neighborhood of $\operatorname{supp}f$ and with $1-\rho$ compactly supported in $I$. On the fixed dense subspace $\underline A_N^{-1}N$, consider $$T(z)\xi=\Phi(a,z)\underline A_N\xi.$$ The localized part of Lemma <a href="#lem:transport-cutoff" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transport-cutoff">9</a>, together with <a href="#eq:transport-domination" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-domination">[eq:transport-domination]</a>, gives a bounded extension satisfying $$\label{eq:charged-collar}
 \|T(z)\|\leq C(1-|z|)^{-d}$$ throughout an inner collar over this neighborhood. Its values on the dense initial domain are Hilbert-valued holomorphic functions, by <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[TenerFusion](#ref-TenerFusion), Lemma 2.9 and Proposition 3.11]. Local uniform boundedness therefore gives operator-norm holomorphy.

For neutral fields, annulus regularization and the Banach-valued boundary argument appear in <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[HTVOA](#ref-HTVOA), Proposition 8.4 and Lemma 8.5]. The charged collar estimate <a href="#eq:charged-collar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:charged-collar">[eq:charged-collar]</a> supplies the hypothesis needed here. We spell out the boundary argument. Put $$F_f(t)=\int f(e^{i\vartheta})e^{id\vartheta}
                         T(te^{i\vartheta})\,\frac{d\vartheta}{2\pi}.$$ All integrals here use the chosen argument and the compact support of $f$. Holomorphy gives $t\partial_tT=-i\partial_\vartheta T$. After integrating by parts, every radial derivative of order $p$ of $F_f$ is a finite sum of integrals of $T$ against derivatives of the fixed smooth test, multiplied by bounded powers of $t^{-1}$. Thus $$\|F_f^{(p)}(t)\|\leq C_{f,p}(1-t)^{-d}.$$ Choose an integer $p>d$. Taylor’s integral formula from a fixed $t_0<1$ expresses $F_f(t)$ as a polynomial plus the integral of $(t-u)^{p-1}F_f^{(p)}(u)/(p-1)!$. The norm of the latter integrand is bounded, uniformly for $u\leq t$, by a constant times $(1-u)^{p-1-d}$, which is integrable at one. Dominated convergence proves that $F_f(t)$ has an operator-norm limit $T_f$ as $t$ tends to one.

On $\underline A_N^{-1}N$, the mode expansion and its polynomial bounds identify this limit with $\Phi(a,f)\underline A_N$. For arbitrary $\xi\in\mathcal H_N$, approximate $\xi$ by $\xi_j\in\underline A_N^{-1}N$. Then $\underline A_N\xi_j\to\underline A_N\xi$ and $T_f\xi_j\to T_f\xi$. Closedness of the smeared field proves <a href="#eq:charged-bounded-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:charged-bounded-cutoff">[eq:charged-bounded-cutoff]</a> on all of $\mathcal H_N$. Lemma <a href="#lem:annulus-range" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:annulus-range">6</a> supplies the asserted range and density.

The product in <a href="#eq:charged-bounded-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:charged-bounded-cutoff">[eq:charged-bounded-cutoff]</a> preserves smooth vectors, since both its factors do. If $\Phi(a,f)^\dagger$ denotes the formal adjoint smear, then every smooth target vector $\eta$ belongs to the adjoint domain of $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$, and $$T_f^*\eta=\underline A_N^*\Phi(a,f)^\dagger\eta.$$ Indeed this equality follows by pairing with the dense finite-energy core and using the adjoint coefficient bounds. The right side is smooth: the formal adjoint smear preserves smooth vectors and the fixed annulus adjoint preserves every Sobolev space. This proves the remaining domain assertion. Finally, <a href="#eq:creation-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:creation-transport">[eq:creation-transport]</a> supplies <a href="#eq:transport-domination" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-domination">[eq:transport-domination]</a> with constant one for creation, with the vacuum as its integrated source. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:integration"></a>
### Integrating the unitary modules

We now construct the local algebra actions on a unitary module. The input is the bounded regularization of a charged field from Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>. We first show that this regularization intertwines the observables in a disjoint interval as closed operators. Creation fields will then identify the observable algebra on the module with the vacuum algebra. Only the already given positive form on the module is used in this construction.

<a id="eq:diagonal-observable-algebra"></a>
For unitary modules $N,K$ and an interval $J$, define the diagonal observable algebra $$\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)=W^*\!\left\{
 \overline{Y^N(v,g)}\oplus\overline{Y^K(v,g)}:
 v\in V\text{ homogeneous},\ 
 g\in C_c^\infty(J)\right\}.
 \label{eq:diagonal-observable-algebra}$$ As for $A_V$, an unbounded generator means its closed graph data, or equivalently its bounded transform and polar data. If $T:\mathcal H_N\to\mathcal H_K$ is a closed densely defined operator, its closed lower-corner operator on $\mathcal H_N\oplus\mathcal H_K$ is $$\widetilde T=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\T&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad\mathop{\mathrm{Dom}}\widetilde T=\mathop{\mathrm{Dom}}T\oplus\mathcal H_K.$$ We say that $T$ *strongly intertwines the observables in $J$* when $\widetilde T$ is affiliated with $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)'$.

<a id="stmt-prop-strong-intertwining"></a>
<div id="prop:strong-intertwining" class="proposition">

**Proposition 11** (Strong intertwining from annulus regularization). *Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, and let $M,N,K$ be grading-restricted unitary $V$-modules, with $N$ strongly integrable. Let $\Phi$ be an intertwining operator of type $\binom{K}{M\,N}$, and let $a\in M$ be homogeneous. Assume that $\Phi(a,z)$ satisfies the transport domination hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>. Smear $\Phi(a,z)$ against a smooth test $f$ supported in an interval $I$, with a fixed argument branch. If $J$ is disjoint from $I$, then the closed smeared operator $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$ strongly intertwines the diagonal smeared observables of $N$ and $K$ supported in $J$. This holds for every homogeneous observable in $V$ and for every such choice of tests and argument branches.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-strong-intertwining"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-strong-intertwining</summary>

*Proof.* Write $\mathcal D_N=\mathcal H_N^\infty$ and $\mathcal D_K=\mathcal H_K^\infty$. The polynomial mode estimates imply that smeared fields and their formal adjoints map the corresponding smooth spaces continuously to smooth spaces. Spectral truncations converge in all the smooth seminorms. Consequently a smear has the same closure whether it is initially defined on finite-energy vectors or on smooth vectors. These facts apply both to the observables and, by Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>, to the charged field.

<a id="eq:bounded-charge-regularization"></a>
Choose the localized annulus $\underline A$ in that lemma with its dent containing $\operatorname{supp}f$ and localized in $I$. Its action $\underline A_N$ is a bounded injective operator with dense range, and $$T_f=\overline{\Phi(a,f)}\underline A_N
 \label{eq:bounded-charge-regularization}$$ is bounded on all of $\mathcal H_N$. Both $T_f$ and $T_f^*$ preserve the respective smooth spaces. On $\mathcal D_K$ the adjoint is $\underline A_N^*\Phi(a,f)^\dagger$, where the dagger denotes the formal smeared adjoint. These are assertions about the full bounded regularization, not merely about its restriction to finite energies.

For homogeneous $v\in V$ and a smooth $g$ supported in $J$, put $$S_N=\overline{Y^N(v,g)},\qquad
 S_K=\overline{Y^K(v,g)}.$$ On the smooth spaces, the intertwining-operator Jacobi identity gives $$Y^K(v,g)\Phi(a,f)=\Phi(a,f)Y^N(v,g).$$ To pass from the formal identity to this equality, multiply the two field orderings by a sufficiently high integral power of the coordinate difference. Polynomial energy bounds make the resulting series smooth-domain distributions. Since the two test supports are disjoint, one can divide the product test by that power. The same argument applies to the adjoint relation, whose observable test has the same support. This is the weak intertwining property in <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Theorem 3.5].

<a id="eq:bounded-charge-core-commutation"></a>
The operator $\underline A_N$ and its adjoint commute strongly with these observables: $\underline A_N$ is localized in $I$, whereas $N$ is already integrated and the observables are localized in $J$. Combining this with the weak identities gives $$S_KT_f\xi=T_fS_N\xi\quad(\xi\in\mathcal D_N),
 \qquad
 S_NT_f^*\eta=T_f^*S_K\eta\quad(\eta\in\mathcal D_K).
 \label{eq:bounded-charge-core-commutation}$$ For the second equality one can pair the first weak intertwining identity for the *formal adjoint observable*. More explicitly, let $S_N^\dagger$ and $S_K^\dagger$ denote its actions on the smooth spaces. The invariant adjoint formula expresses it as a finite sum of smears of PCT-transformed states and their $L_1$-descendants, with tests still supported in $J$. Thus the first equality also gives $T_fS_N^\dagger=S_K^\dagger T_f$ on $\mathcal D_N$. For $\xi\in\mathcal D_N$ and $\eta\in\mathcal D_K$, using our linear-first inner product convention, we obtain $$\begin{aligned}
 \langle S_NT_f^*\eta,\xi\rangle
 &=\langle T_f^*\eta,S_N^\dagger\xi\rangle
  =\langle\eta,T_fS_N^\dagger\xi\rangle\\
 &=\langle\eta,S_K^\dagger T_f\xi\rangle
  =\langle S_K\eta,T_f\xi\rangle
  =\langle T_f^*S_K\eta,\xi\rangle.
\end{aligned}$$ All vectors in these pairings are smooth. This proves the second equality in <a href="#eq:bounded-charge-core-commutation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-charge-core-commutation">[eq:bounded-charge-core-commutation]</a> without identifying a formal-adjoint closure with the full Hilbert-space adjoint.

We spell out the passage to closed operators. On $\mathcal H_N\oplus\mathcal H_K$, let $$D=S_N\oplus S_K,\qquad
 Q_f=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\T_f&0\end{pmatrix}.$$ Approximation in the graph norms, using the smooth cores, shows that both $Q_f$ and $Q_f^*$ preserve $\operatorname{Dom}D$ and commute with $D$ there. Hence $Q_f\oplus Q_f$ and its adjoint preserve the closed graph of $D$. They therefore commute with its orthogonal projection. The matrix entries of this graph projection determine the usual bounded transform and polar data of $D$. Thus $Q_f$ commutes with the von Neumann algebra generated by $D$.

As $v$ and $g$ vary, the preceding argument proves $Q_f\in\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)'$.

It remains to remove the annulus. The first-summand compression of $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)$ is $\pi_J^N(A_V(J))$: its normal range is generated by the module smears, which are the images of the vacuum generators under strong integration. Since $\underline A_N$ is localized in $I$, both $\operatorname{diag}(\underline A_N,1)$ and its adjoint commute with $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)$. The closed inverse $\underline A_N^{-1}$ is densely defined on $\operatorname{ran}\underline A_N$, and inversion preserves the resulting affiliation with the commutant. Therefore $$Q_f\begin{pmatrix}\underline A_N^{-1}&0\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$$ is densely defined, and its domain and action commute with every unitary in $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)$. It is closable: by <a href="#eq:bounded-charge-regularization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-charge-regularization">[eq:bounded-charge-regularization]</a> it is a restriction of the closed operator with lower corner $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$ and all other corners zero. Its domain contains the finite-energy core in the first summand, since $N\subset\operatorname{ran}\underline A_N$ by Lemma <a href="#lem:annulus-range" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:annulus-range">6</a>. Its closure is consequently exactly that closed lower-corner operator. This proves its affiliation with $\mathcal B_{N,K}(J)'$.

This affiliation is the strong intertwining assertion, including the diagram convention used in <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Section 3.3]. Explicitly, on two copies of $\mathcal H_N\oplus\mathcal H_K$, repeat the charged corner diagonally and place a diagonal observable in a matrix corner between the copies. The latter is affiliated with $M_2(\mathcal B_{N,K}(J))$, and the repeated charged corner with its commutant. Hence the diagram commutes strongly. All operations above are valid for each fixed branch on $I$. ◻

</details>

We next use creation fields to show that the vacuum compression of a diagonal observable algebra loses no information.

<a id="stmt-lem-creation-totality"></a>
<div id="lem:creation-totality" class="lemma">

**Lemma 12** (Totality of local creation ranges). *Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational, let $M$ be a grading-restricted unitary $V$-module, and let $I$ be a nonempty interval. For every homogeneous $a\in M$ and $f\in C_c^\infty(I)$, form the bounded creation regularization $$T_{a,f}=\overline{\mathcal Y_M^+(a,f)}\underline A_V$$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>; the localized annulus may depend on the test. The closed linear span of their ranges is $\mathcal H_M$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-creation-totality"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-creation-totality</summary>

*Proof.* The transport domination needed here holds with equality for creation and vacuum source. It uses the given form on $M$, and assumes neither integration of $M$ nor positivity of a general fusion product. Since $\Omega\in\operatorname{ran}\underline A_V$, each range contains $\mathcal Y_M^+(a,f)\Omega$.

Suppose $\eta\in\mathcal H_M$ is orthogonal to all these vectors. For each homogeneous $a$, consider $$F_a(z)=\langle e^{zL_{-1}}a,\eta\rangle,
 \qquad |z|<1.$$ Creation gives $\mathcal Y_M^+(a,z)\Omega=e^{zL_{-1}}a$. The charged energy estimate bounds the Taylor coefficients of $F_a$ by a polynomial in their index. Its boundary value is therefore a distribution with only nonnegative Fourier frequencies. Orthogonality to all the creation smears says that this distribution vanishes on $I$. The smooth weight or branch factor in the smearing convention can be absorbed into the test on this interval.

For completeness, such a one-sided distribution is zero if it vanishes on a nonempty arc. Convolve it with smooth approximate identities supported in increasingly small arcs. Each convolution still has only nonnegative frequencies and vanishes on a smaller nonempty arc. It is the smooth boundary value of a holomorphic function in the disc. The Schwarz reflection principle across an arc on which that function is zero, followed by analytic uniqueness, shows that the convolution is identically zero. Passing to the distributional limit proves the claim. Thus $F_a=0$, and its constant coefficient gives $\langle a,\eta\rangle=0$. Homogeneous vectors span $M$, which is dense in $\mathcal H_M$; hence $\eta=0$. ◻

</details>

Local-intertwiner criteria for module integration are given in <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[GuiUnitaryII](#ref-GuiUnitaryII), Proposition 4.7] and <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Lemma 4.27]. The following diagonal-algebra proof uses the bounded creation operators and their total ranges to establish the representations together with equality of the full closed graphs.

<a id="stmt-thm-integration"></a>
<div id="thm:integration" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:exact-cwx-smear"></a>
**Theorem 13** (Strong integration of every unitary module). *Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational. Every grading-restricted unitary $V$-module $M$ has a unique compatible family of normal representations $$\pi_J^M:A_V(J)\longrightarrow B(\mathcal H_M)$$ such that, for every homogeneous $v\in V$ and $g\in C_c^\infty(J)$, $$\pi_J^M\!\left(\overline{Y^V(v,g)}\right)
       =\overline{Y^M(v,g)}.
 \label{eq:exact-cwx-smear}$$ Equality here includes the domains of the closed affiliated operators. The canonical Virasoro action of this net representation is the integrated module Virasoro action; in particular its rotations are $e^{itL_0}$ with the module’s conformal-energy normalization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-integration"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-integration</summary>

*Proof.* Module energy bounds follow from Theorem <a href="#thm:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:energy">2</a>, and the vacuum net exists by Corollary <a href="#cor:locality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:locality">5</a>. Fix $J$ and form $\mathcal B(J)=\mathcal B_{V,M}(J)$ as in <a href="#eq:diagonal-observable-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:diagonal-observable-algebra">[eq:diagonal-observable-algebra]</a>, including every homogeneous observable. Its two Hilbert summands reduce all its generators. Vacuum restriction is therefore a normal unital homomorphism $$r_0:\mathcal B(J)\longrightarrow A_V(J).$$ It is onto. Indeed, compression of the graph data of a direct sum gives the graph data of its first summand, and these compressed data generate $A_V(J)$. The range of a normal homomorphism of von Neumann algebras is a von Neumann algebra, so it contains that generated algebra and is equal to it.

The kernel of $r_0$ is $\mathcal B(J)q$ for a central projection $q$. Choose any interval $I$ disjoint from $J$. Apply Proposition <a href="#prop:strong-intertwining" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:strong-intertwining">11</a> to creation, whose source is the integrated vacuum. Its bounded regularizations commute, as lower corners, with $\mathcal B(J)$. Since $q$ annihilates the vacuum summand, its restriction $q_M$ to the other summand satisfies $$q_MT_{a,f}=T_{a,f}q_V=0.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:creation-totality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:creation-totality">12</a> gives $q_M=0$, hence $q=0$. Thus $r_0$ is a normal isomorphism. Its inverse is normal as well: an order isomorphism preserves suprema of bounded increasing positive nets. If $r_M$ denotes restriction to the module summand, define $$\pi_J^M=r_Mr_0^{-1}.$$ For $J_1\subset J_2$ the diagonal algebras are nested, and both compression maps respect the inclusions. It follows that $\pi_{J_2}^M|_{A_V(J_1)}=\pi_{J_1}^M$.

We verify the exact closed-operator identity. Let $S_V=\overline{Y^V(v,g)}$ and $S_M=\overline{Y^M(v,g)}$. The graph projection of $S_V\oplus S_M$ has its matrix entries in $\mathcal B(J)$. Compressing its entries to the two summands yields the graph projections of $S_V$ and $S_M$, respectively. Consequently the matrix amplification of $\pi_J^M$ sends the entire graph projection of $S_V$ to that of $S_M$. This is precisely the normal image of an affiliated closed operator, and proves <a href="#eq:exact-cwx-smear" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-cwx-smear">[eq:exact-cwx-smear]</a>. In particular the argument identifies closed graphs, rather than merely operators on a dense common domain.

The graph data of the vacuum smears generate each $A_V(J)$, so the same identity also proves uniqueness. With the module energy bounds, it is the defining property of strong integrability and of the CWX action; see <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Definition 4.22]. Applying the identity to the conformal vector identifies all localized stress-energy fields. Their canonical centrally extended Virasoro actions agree by <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Proposition 3.10], equivalently <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Remark 4.23]. This gives exactly $e^{itL_0}$ for rotations, not an arbitrarily shifted positive-energy generator. The normalization is important when using $r^{L_0}$ in annulus factorizations. ◻

</details>

The construction respects finite orthogonal sums. This follows either from the closed-smear identity and uniqueness, or directly from the diagonal construction. Thus the theorem applies to all objects of the grading-restricted unitary category, including finite sums of the vacuum. It makes no assertion yet that every simple algebraic module admits such a positive form. We establish that remaining assertion after proving positivity of transport for pairs of unitary modules.

<a id="sec:fusion"></a>
### Positive forms on fusion products

We now use the normal representations constructed in Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a> to transfer a vacuum Gram matrix to an arbitrary unitary module. The algebraic fusion product need not yet be unitary. We first construct a nondegenerate invariant sesquilinear form on that product, and then prove its positivity separately on every conformal-energy space.

<a id="eq:two-point-transport"></a>
<a id="eq:positive-transport-region"></a>
For unitary modules $M,N$, extend the diagonal transport form <a href="#eq:transport-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-form">[eq:transport-form]</a> to positive real insertion points by setting $$\begin{split}
 \mathfrak t^{M,N}_{z,w}(a\otimes b,a'\otimes b')
 =\big\langle
 Y^N\big(\mathcal Y_M^-(\theta_w a',w^{-1}-z)a,z\big)b,b'
 \big\rangle_N,
\end{split}
\label{eq:two-point-transport}$$ where $a,a'\in M$, $b,b'\in N$, and $$0<z,w<1,\qquad w^{-1}>z>w^{-1}-z>0.
 \label{eq:positive-transport-region}$$ As usual, insertion of a vector in the grade completion means the absolutely convergent sum over its homogeneous components. Thus $\mathfrak t^{M,N}_{r,r}=\mathfrak t^{M,N}_r$ in the notation of Section <a href="#sec:annuli" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:annuli">4</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-positive-fusion"></a>
<div id="thm:positive-fusion" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:positive-fusion-identity"></a>
**Theorem 14** (Positive fusion forms). *Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. For any unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules $M,N$, let $X=M\boxtimes N$ and let $\mathcal Y_X$ be its universal intertwining operator. There is a positive-definite invariant Hermitian form $h_X$ on $X$ such that $$\mathfrak t^{M,N}_{z,w}(a\otimes b,a'\otimes b')
 =\sum_\lambda h_X\big(
 \Pi_\lambda\mathcal Y_X(a,z)b,
 \Pi_\lambda\mathcal Y_X(a',w)b'\big)
 \label{eq:positive-fusion-identity}$$ for all the vectors and coordinates in <a href="#eq:two-point-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-point-transport">[eq:two-point-transport]</a>–<a href="#eq:positive-transport-region" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-transport-region">[eq:positive-transport-region]</a>. The sum converges absolutely. This is the canonical positive normalization of the fusion form.*

</div>

We prove the theorem in the next three subsections and check its normalization in the fourth.

<a id="pm-h-24"></a>
#### A positive matrix in the local algebra

We begin with positivity of the diagonal form. Fix an interval $I$ near $1\in S^1$, a localized dented annulus $\underline A$ as in Section <a href="#sec:annuli" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:annuli">4</a>, and its double $\widehat A=\underline A^\dagger\underline A$. Choose a real $r<1$ sufficiently close to $1$ in its inner collar. This choice works for every finite list of charge vectors; constants in estimates may depend on that list. Write $\delta=r^{-1}-r$ and $q=2\delta$.

For $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in M$, set $$u_{ij}=\mathcal Y_M^-(\theta_r a_i,\delta)a_j
 \quad\text{in the grade completion of }V.$$ By linearity and <a href="#eq:annulus-hilbert-state" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:annulus-hilbert-state">[eq:annulus-hilbert-state]</a>, $q^{-L_0}u_{ij}\in\mathcal H_V$. The fixed-disc estimate <a href="#eq:fixed-disc-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fixed-disc-bound">[eq:fixed-disc-bound]</a> therefore defines bounded operators <a id="eq:local-transport-matrix"></a>$$K_{ij}=\widehat A_V[u_{ij}(r)]\in A_V(I)
 \label{eq:local-transport-matrix}$$ as operator-norm limits of the insertions of the finite level truncations of $u_{ij}$. In particular these limits remain in the same local von Neumann algebra.

The vacuum transport identity for creation, namely Proposition 3.10 of <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[TenerFusion](#ref-TenerFusion)], and the insertion identity <a href="#eq:annulus-insertion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:annulus-insertion">[eq:annulus-insertion]</a> give <a id="eq:transport-gram"></a>$$K_{ij}=T_i(r)^*T_j(r),\qquad
 T_j(r)=\mathcal Y_M^+(a_j,r)\underline A_V.
 \label{eq:transport-gram}$$ Here the creation operators with the displayed cutoff are bounded by Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>. The equality is first an equality of matrix elements on $\underline A_V^{-1}V$, which is dense by Lemma <a href="#lem:annulus-range" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:annulus-range">6</a>; boundedness extends it to the Hilbert space. Thus $(K_{ij})_{i,j=1}^m$ is a positive element of $M_m(A_V(I))$.

<a id="eq:transport-positive"></a>
By Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a>, $N$ has a normal local representation $\pi_I^N$. Applying its matrix amplification preserves this positivity. For arbitrary $b_1,\ldots,b_m\in N$, choose exact preimages $\xi_i$ under $\underline A_N$ as follows. If the complementary annulus of Lemma <a href="#lem:annulus-range" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:annulus-range">6</a> satisfies $\underline A_N\underline B_N=s^{L_0}$, put $$\xi_i=\underline B_Ns^{-L_0}b_i.$$ The inverse power acts on a finite-energy vector, so this expression is defined and $\underline A_N\xi_i=b_i$. The norm-convergent insertion identity, followed by absolute convergence of the transport series, now gives $$\sum_{i,j=1}^m
 \mathfrak t^{M,N}_{r,r}(a_j\otimes b_j,a_i\otimes b_i)
 =\sum_{i,j=1}^m
 \langle\pi_I^N(K_{ij})\xi_j,\xi_i\rangle_N
 \geq0.
 \label{eq:transport-positive}$$ Only the creation case was used to obtain the vacuum matrix. General fusion positivity has not entered this argument.

<a id="pm-h-25"></a>
#### The invariant form on the algebraic fusion object

<a id="eq:fused-evaluation"></a>
<a id="eq:rigid-transport-adjunction"></a>
Assume first that $M,N$ are simple. Associativity in the positive-real region produces an intertwining operator $B$ of type $\binom{N}{\bar M\ X}$ such that $$Y^N\big(\mathcal Y_M^-(c,w^{-1}-z)a,z\big)b
 =B(c,w^{-1})\mathcal Y_X(a,z)b
 \label{eq:fused-evaluation}$$ in convergent matrix elements, for $c\in\bar M$, $a\in M$ and $b\in N$. Its morphism is the associator followed by the annihilation morphism $\bar M\boxtimes M\longrightarrow V$ and the left unit action on $N$. The annihilation is nonzero: its adjoint is creation, which sends $a\otimes\Omega$ to $e^{zL_{-1}}a$. Since $M$ is simple, unitarity identifies $\bar M$ with $M^\vee$, and the annihilation morphism is a nonzero multiple of rigid evaluation. We also write $B$ for its corresponding morphism. Consequently rigidity gives an isomorphism $$\operatorname{Hom}_V(E,X)\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}_V(\bar M\boxtimes E,N),
 \qquad f\longmapsto B(1_{\bar M}\boxtimes f)
 \label{eq:rigid-transport-adjunction}$$ for every grading-restricted finite-length module $E$.

To turn <a href="#eq:fused-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fused-evaluation">[eq:fused-evaluation]</a> into a form, recall the Hermitian duality from Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>, which does not require positive forms. For a module $E$, its conjugate $\bar E$ has mode action $$v_{(n)}^{\bar E}\bar x
   =\overline{(\Theta v)_{(n)}^E x}.$$ Its Hermitian dual is $E^{\mathrm h}=(\bar E)^\vee$, with pairing <a id="eq:hermitian-dual-pairing"></a>$$_E=\overline{\lambda(\bar x)}.
 \label{eq:hermitian-dual-pairing}$$ The pairing is linear in $x$ and conjugate-linear in $\lambda$. Restricted contragredience gives its invariant adjoint relation. The given inner product of $N$ is represented by a linear module isomorphism $H_N:N\longrightarrow N^{\mathrm h}$.

<a id="eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint"></a>
Conjugate $B$ and apply the FHL contragredient transformation <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[FHL](#ref-FHL), Section 5.5], in the explicit algebraic form <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[HLZII](#ref-HLZII), Equation (3.87), Proposition 3.46], taking the integer branch index in that formula to be $-1$. Thus we use $$e^{wL_1}e^{-i\pi L_0}w^{-2L_0}$$ on the conjugated charge, and precompose the second input with $H_N$. This gives an intertwining operator $B^\sharp$ of type $\binom{X^{\mathrm h}}{M\ N}$ with $$_X
   =\langle B(\theta_w a,w^{-1})x,b\rangle_N
 \qquad(x\in X,\ a\in M,\ b\in N, w>0).
 \label{eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint}$$ The minus phase before conjugation gives the plus phase in $\theta_w$. All module weights are real, so these transformations also preserve the prescribed positive-real branches. Universality of $\mathcal Y_X$ gives a module map $H:X\longrightarrow X^{\mathrm h}$ such that $B^\sharp=H\mathcal Y_X$.

The map $H$ is surjective. To see the role of rigidity, suppose that the quotient map $q:X^{\mathrm h}\longrightarrow C=\operatorname{coker}H$ has nonzero target. Hermitian duality identifies $(X^{\mathrm h})^{\mathrm h}$ with $X$, so dualizing $q$ gives a nonzero injection $$f=q^{\mathrm h}:E=C^{\mathrm h}\longrightarrow X,
 \qquad f^{\mathrm h}H=qH=0.$$ Pairing <a href="#eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint">[eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint]</a> with $f(E)$ would imply $B(1_{\bar M}\boxtimes f)=0$: the charge transformation $\theta_w$ is invertible, and the pairing with $N$ is nondegenerate. This contradicts <a href="#eq:rigid-transport-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rigid-transport-adjunction">[eq:rigid-transport-adjunction]</a>. Hermitian duality preserves finite length, so the surjective map $H$ is an isomorphism.

<a id="eq:algebraic-fusion-form"></a>
We have therefore constructed the nondegenerate invariant sesquilinear form $$h_X(x,y)=[x,Hy]_X.
 \label{eq:algebraic-fusion-form}$$ It pairs distinct energy spaces to zero. At this stage neither Hermitian symmetry nor positivity has been assumed. Combining <a href="#eq:fused-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fused-evaluation">[eq:fused-evaluation]</a> and <a href="#eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint">[eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint]</a> proves <a href="#eq:positive-fusion-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-fusion-identity">[eq:positive-fusion-identity]</a> for this form. Absolute convergence is the ordinary convergence of the product of $B$ and $\mathcal Y_X$, applied to the restricted-dual functional supplied by a finite-energy vector of $N$.

<a id="pm-h-26"></a>
#### Extracting positivity at one energy

The positivity in <a href="#eq:transport-positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-positive">[eq:transport-positive]</a> concerns a sum over all output energies. The following lemma separates those energies without first constructing a norm on $X$.

<a id="stmt-lem-positive-moments"></a>
<div id="lem:positive-moments" class="lemma">

**Lemma 15** (Positive polynomial moments). *Let $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R$ be countable and bounded below. Suppose complex numbers $c_\lambda$ satisfy $$\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}e^{\epsilon\lambda}|c_\lambda|<\infty
 \quad\text{for some }\epsilon>0.$$ If $\sum_\lambda |p(\lambda)|^2c_\lambda$ is real and nonnegative for every complex polynomial $p$, then every $c_\lambda$ is real and nonnegative.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-positive-moments"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-positive-moments</summary>

*Proof.* Set $\mu=\sum_\lambda c_\lambda\delta_\lambda$. Polynomials are dense in $L^2(|\mu|)$. To see this, let $g$ be orthogonal to them. Cauchy–Schwarz and the exponential moment show that the finite complex measure $g\,|\mu|$ has an exponential moment of smaller positive order. Its bilateral Laplace transform is holomorphic in a strip containing the imaginary axis. Orthogonality makes every derivative at zero vanish, so the identity theorem makes the transform zero on this strip. Uniqueness of the Fourier transform of a finite measure then gives $g\,|\mu|=0$. For completeness, this last uniqueness follows by convolving the measure with Gaussian approximate identities: every convolution has zero Fourier transform and is zero, and the convolutions converge weakly to the measure.

For a fixed $\lambda$, approximate the indicator of its atom by polynomials $p_n$ in $L^2(|\mu|)$. Then $|p_n|^2$ converges to that indicator in $L^1(|\mu|)$, because $$\big\||p_n|^2-|p_m|^2\big\|_{L^1(|\mu|)}
 \leq\|p_n-p_m\|_{L^2(|\mu|)}
       \big(\|p_n\|_{L^2(|\mu|)}+\|p_m\|_{L^2(|\mu|)}\big).$$ Thus the nonnegative integrals $\int |p_n|^2\,d\mu$ tend to $c_\lambda$. ◻

</details>

Fix the radius $r$ used in <a href="#eq:transport-positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-positive">[eq:transport-positive]</a>. Consider a finite sum in the grade completion, $$x=\sum_i\mathcal Y_X(a_i,r)b_i,
 \qquad x_\lambda=\Pi_\lambda x,
 \qquad c_\lambda=h_X(x_\lambda,x_\lambda).$$ After decomposing the inputs, we may take all $a_i,b_i$ homogeneous. The numbers $c_\lambda$ have an exponential moment in absolute value. Indeed, choose $z>r$ sufficiently close to $r$ that $z<r^{-1}$. In the absolutely convergent product with the outer insertion fixed at $r^{-1}$, replacing the inner radius $r$ by $z$ multiplies its component of weight $\lambda$ by $$(z/r)^{\lambda-\operatorname{wt}(a_i)-\operatorname{wt}(b_i)}.$$ Absolute product convergence for each of the finitely many pairs of inputs gives $\sum_\lambda e^{\epsilon\lambda}|c_\lambda|<\infty$ for a common $\epsilon>0$. This uses only the sesquilinear form <a href="#eq:algebraic-fusion-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebraic-fusion-form">[eq:algebraic-fusion-form]</a> and algebraic convergence.

The space of such finite sums $x$ is stable under $L_0$, since <a id="eq:fixed-radius-L0"></a>$$L_0\mathcal Y_X(a,r)b
 =\mathcal Y_X(L_0a,r)b+\mathcal Y_X(a,r)L_0b
   +r\mathcal Y_X(L_{-1}a,r)b.
 \label{eq:fixed-radius-L0}$$ The radius $r$ stays fixed in this step. Each polynomial in $L_0$ introduces only finitely many further charge and source vectors, and the fixed-disc construction of <a href="#eq:transport-positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-positive">[eq:transport-positive]</a> applies at this same $r$ to every such finite list. Its constants may change; its positivity conclusion does not. Apply <a href="#eq:transport-positive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-positive">[eq:transport-positive]</a> to $p(L_0)x$. Grade orthogonality gives $$\sum_\lambda |p(\lambda)|^2h_X(x_\lambda,x_\lambda)\geq0.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:positive-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:positive-moments">15</a> proves $h_X(x_\lambda,x_\lambda)\geq0$ at each energy.

These $x_\lambda$ run through the whole space $X_\lambda$. In fact the span of all coefficients of $\mathcal Y_X(a,z)b$ is a graded submodule by the intertwiner commutator formula. The quotient map by that submodule has zero postcomposition with the universal intertwiner, and is therefore zero. For homogeneous inputs each projection at the fixed nonzero radius $r$ is a nonzero scalar multiple of its coefficient. This proves the asserted spanning at each energy.

It follows by polarization that $h_X$ is Hermitian and positive semidefinite on every energy space, and hence on $X$. A zero-norm vector for a positive semidefinite Hermitian form belongs to its radical. Nondegeneracy of <a href="#eq:algebraic-fusion-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:algebraic-fusion-form">[eq:algebraic-fusion-form]</a> therefore makes $h_X$ positive definite. In particular, $X$ is now a unitary module. Finite orthogonal decompositions of $M,N$ extend the construction to arbitrary unitary finite-length modules: creation, annihilation and the transport identity split in exactly these orthogonal blocks.

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### Canonical normalization and general charged fields

With $h_X$ now positive, <a href="#eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint">[eq:hermitian-dual-adjoint]</a> says that $B$ is the adjoint of $\mathcal Y_X$ in Tener’s convention. Thus <a href="#eq:fused-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fused-evaluation">[eq:fused-evaluation]</a> is the exact normalized fusion identity for that convention.

We check the normalization, since fractional charge weights make the adjoint phase significant. Put, for $x>0$, $$U_\pm(x)=e^{xL_1}e^{\pm i\pi L_0}x^{-2L_0}.$$ Gui’s contragredient convention is $U_+(x)$ in <a id="pm-cite-69"></a>[[GuiPositivity](#ref-GuiPositivity), Equations (8.3)–(8.5)]; solving for the adjoint uses $U_-(x)=U_+(x^{-1})^{-1}$. On a simple charge module of lowest weight $h$, $U_-=e^{-2\pi i h}U_+$. With inner products linear in the first variable, this means that Gui’s adjoint, after the unitary identification of the conjugate charge with the contragredient charge, is $e^{2\pi i h}$ times Tener’s adjoint. Gui’s annihilation operator has the same factor relative to $\mathcal Y_M^-$: its vacuum coefficient on a top vector $a$ is $e^{i\pi h}x^{-2h}\|a\|^2$, whereas the coefficient for $\mathcal Y_M^-$ is $e^{-i\pi h}x^{-2h}\|a\|^2$.

The two factors cancel in the fusion identity <a id="pm-cite-70"></a>[[GuiPositivity](#ref-GuiPositivity), Equation (8.8)]. Thus the canonical operator called $A$ in that identity is $1$ for the form just constructed. The identity leaves no scalar freedom: the coefficients of the universal intertwiner span $X$, and their pairings determine its form. This proves the canonical-normalization assertion in Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>. The comparison applies on each orthogonal simple charge summand, so also covers finite sums. Common-angle versions follow by simultaneous $L_0$ rotations, using conjugate logarithms at reflected coordinates. In particular <a href="#eq:positive-fusion-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-fusion-identity">[eq:positive-fusion-identity]</a> at a rotated diagonal is the squared norm of the universal intertwiner there.

We have proved closure of the unitary modules under the full algebraic fusion product, without yet knowing that every simple module is unitary. The following consequence supplies the charged fields needed for the comparison with conformal nets.

<a id="stmt-cor-charged-general"></a>
<div id="cor:charged-general" class="corollary">

**Corollary 16**. *Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA, and let $M,N,K$ be unitary grading-restricted finite-length $V$-modules. Every intertwining operator $\Phi$ of type $\binom{K}{M\ N}$ is polynomially energy-bounded for every homogeneous charge vector and has the strong intertwining property with all observable fields. More precisely, for homogeneous $a\in M$ of weight $d$, write $$\Phi(a,z)=\sum_\mu\Phi(a)[\mu]z^{-\mu-d}.$$ There are $C>0$ and nonnegative integers $s,k$ such that $$\|\Phi(a)[\mu]b\|
 \leq C(1+|\mu|)^s\|(1+L_0)^k b\|
 \qquad(b\in N).$$ If a smooth test $f$ is supported in a lifted interval $I$, the closed off-diagonal smear $\overline{\Phi(a,f)}$ on $\mathcal H_N\oplus\mathcal H_K$ is affiliated with the commutant of the algebra generated by $$\operatorname{diag}\big(\overline{Y^N(v,g)},
                         \overline{Y^K(v,g)}\big),
 \qquad v\in V,\quad \operatorname{supp}g\subset J,$$ for every interval $J$ disjoint from $I$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-charged-general"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-charged-general</summary>

*Proof.* By universality, $\Phi=L\mathcal Y_X$ for a module map $L:X=M\boxtimes N\longrightarrow K$. Both modules are now unitary. Such a map is bounded: decompose into the finitely many unitary simple summands; Schur’s lemma and uniqueness of an invariant form on each simple identify it with a finite family of linear maps between finite-dimensional multiplicity spaces. Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>, including its rotated-diagonal identity, gives $$\|\Phi(a,z)b\|^2
 \leq\|L\|^2\,
       \mathfrak t_z^{M,N}(a\otimes b,a\otimes b).$$ The source $N$ is strongly integrable by Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a>. This is precisely the transport domination hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a>, which gives the stated mode bounds and bounded annulus-cutoff smears. Proposition <a href="#prop:strong-intertwining" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:strong-intertwining">11</a> then gives the asserted affiliation and strong intertwining property for homogeneous observables. For a finite sum of homogeneous observables, sum the weak commutation identities on the common smooth core before closing. The same bounded regularization and its adjoint preserve that core, so the graph argument of that proposition also applies to the closure of the sum. This proves the assertion for every $v\in V$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:unitarity"></a>
### Unitarizing every simple module

Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a> closes the unitary modules under algebraic fusion. We now use this closure to reach every simple module.

<a id="stmt-thm-strong-unitarity"></a>
<div id="thm:strong-unitarity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 17**. *Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational vertex operator algebra. Every simple grading-restricted $V$-module admits a positive definite invariant Hermitian form. Consequently, every grading-restricted finite-length $V$-module is unitarizable, and $V$ is strongly unitary.*

</div>

Here is how a module with no given positive form enters the unitary theory. Fix a simple $V$-module $P$, and set $$D=V\otimes V,\qquad
 U=D^{\langle\sigma\rangle},\qquad R=P\otimes P^\vee,$$ where $\sigma$ exchanges the factors. The transposition-twisted vacuum module $T=T_\sigma(V)$ is unitary over $U$. The permutation fusion formula below produces a nonzero twisted intertwiner of type $\binom{T}{R\ T}$. Its charge map is injective because $R$ is simple over $D$. Consequently it detects every simple constituent of $R|_U$, placing that constituent in the positive fusion product $T\boxtimes_U T^\vee$. This gives a positive $U$-invariant form $h_1$ on $R$.

Restriction to $U$ controls only the flip-invariant fields. To recover the adjoint identity for the remaining fields, write $D=U\oplus J$, where $J=\{d\in D:\sigma(d)=-d\}$ is the sign constituent. Its action identifies $J\boxtimes_U R$ with $R$. Transferring the canonical fusion form along that action gives a second form $h_2$. The normalized transport identity interchanges $h_1$ and $h_2$ when taking the adjoint of a $J$-field, so $h_1+h_2$ is invariant under all of $D$. Its restriction to $P\otimes\mathbb Cq$, for any nonzero $q\in P^\vee$, then unitarizes $P$. We prove the algebraic permutation formula first, without using unitarity of $P$.

<a id="pm-h-29"></a>
#### The algebraic permutation product

<a id="stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity"></a>
<div id="lem:permutation-before-positivity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 18** (The required permutation product before positivity). *Let $V$ be simple and strongly rational, let $D=V\otimes V$, and let $\sigma$ exchange the factors. Write $T_\sigma(N)$ for the Barron–Dong–Mason transposition-twisted module associated to an ordinary $V$-module $N$ <a id="pm-cite-71"></a>[[BDM](#ref-BDM), Theorem 3.9]. The fusion products below are taken among grading-restricted ordinary or $\sigma$-twisted $D$-modules. For every ordinary $V$-module $P$, $$(P\otimes P^\vee)\boxtimes_D T_\sigma(V)
 \cong T_\sigma(P\boxtimes_V P^\vee).$$ No unitarity or positivity of the lowest conformal weight of $P$ is assumed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity</summary>

*Proof.* Put $U=D^{\langle\sigma\rangle}$. Cyclic-orbifold regularity <a id="pm-cite-72"></a>[[CarnahanMiyamoto](#ref-CarnahanMiyamoto), Theorem 5.24 and Corollary 5.25] makes $U$ strongly rational. Let $\mathcal C$ be its category of grading-restricted generalized modules, with its HLZ vertex tensor category structure, and write $c_{X,Y}$ for its braiding. We use $L(n)=L_n$ for Virasoro modes in the coordinate formulas below. Ordinary $D$-modules and the BDM modules $T_\sigma(N)$ restrict to objects of $\mathcal C$: $$L^{T_\sigma(N)}(0)=\tfrac12L^N(0)+\tfrac{c_V}{16}.$$ This requires boundedness below and finite-dimensional weight spaces, not nonnegative module weights.

Regard $D$ as a commutative algebra object in $\mathcal C$, and write $\star$ for the relative product on $\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathcal C}(D)$. This product is the cokernel imposing equality of the two $D$-actions. Right exactness allows the ambient associator and its inverse to descend through these quotients, giving an associator for $\star$ <a id="pm-cite-73"></a>[[CKMExtensions](#ref-CKMExtensions), Theorems 2.51–2.53]. If $\mu_Q$ denotes the action on an object $Q$, the categorical $g$-twisted condition is $$\mu_Q(g\boxtimes 1_Q)c_{Q,D}c_{D,Q}=\mu_Q .$$ For the canonical quotient $q:X\boxtimes_U Y\to Q=X\star Y$ of objects in the $g$- and $h$-sectors, the target lies in the $gh$-sector: precompose this identity with $1_D\boxtimes q$, use the hexagon and balancing, and then apply the two input identities. Right exactness makes that precomposition surjective <a id="pm-cite-74"></a>[[McRaeTwisted](#ref-McRaeTwisted), Proposition 2.4 and Corollary 2.5]. The field attached to $\mu_Q$ has the vacuum, truncation, derivative, and grading properties; the displayed monodromy condition gives the equivariance for its prescribed twist. Analytic associativity reconstructs its twisted Jacobi identity <a id="pm-cite-75"></a>[[McRaeTwisted](#ref-McRaeTwisted), Propositions 4.11 and 4.13, Remark 4.12 and Theorem 4.14]. Thus these are actual twisted modules. Only the known $1$- and $\sigma$-sectors are needed, not an exhaustive classification of algebra modules.

We spell out the relevant intertwiner dictionary. The two categorical action compatibilities are precisely analytic associativity and skew-associativity for the corresponding $U$-intertwiner $\mathcal I$ <a id="pm-cite-76"></a>[[CKMExtensions](#ref-CKMExtensions), Theorem 3.44 and Proposition 3.50]. Suppose its charge $X$ is ordinary and its source and target are $\sigma$-twisted. For $\sigma a=(-1)^j a$ with $j\in\{0,1\}$, the matrix coefficients of $$\begin{gathered}
 Y_{\rm target}(a,z_1)\mathcal I(x,z_2)y,\\
 \mathcal I(x,z_2)Y_{\rm source}(a,z_1)y,\\
 \mathcal I(Y_X(a,z_1-z_2)x,z_2)y
 \end{gathered}$$ are branches of one analytic function $g(z_1,z_2)$. For fixed nonzero $z_2$, multiplication by $z_1^{j/2}$ cancels monodromy about $0$. The iterate has integer powers about $z_1=z_2$, since $X$ is ordinary. These two local loops generate the punctured-plane fundamental group, so $z_1^{j/2}g(z_1,z_2)$ is single-valued. Source truncation gives a pole of finite order at $0$, and ordinary-charge truncation gives one at $z_1=z_2$. At infinity, the finite weight support of the restricted-dual vector and the lower bound on intermediate target weights give polynomial growth. Thus the only possible poles are at $0,z_2,\infty$. To pass from this fixed-$z_2$ rationality to a two-variable formal identity, take homogeneous inputs of weights $h_x,h_y$, a state $a$ of weight $d$, and a restricted-dual vector of weight $H$. On compatible branches, the $L(0)$-bracket gives $$z_1^{j/2}g(z_1,z_2)
 =z_2^{H-d-h_x-h_y+j/2}
   (z_1/z_2)^{j/2}g(z_1/z_2,1).$$ The function $u\mapsto u^{j/2}g(u,1)$ is rational, with poles only at $0,1,\infty$; the prefactor is a rational power. Its three expansions therefore give the twisted Jacobi identity. The converse follows from formal duality <a id="pm-cite-77"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Proposition 2.17]. Semisimple $L(0)$ and the $L(0)$-bracket remove logarithmic terms. Here the factor is $z_1^{j/2}$, as in Proposition 2.17; this corrects the interchange of the two exponents in the displayed cancellation factor in the proof of <a id="pm-cite-78"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Theorem 3.5]. Consequently $\star$ represents exactly the needed twisted intertwining operators. On ordinary modules it is ordinary $D$-fusion <a id="pm-cite-79"></a>[[CKMExtensions](#ref-CKMExtensions), Theorem 3.54 and Corollary 3.55].

For an integer $k\geq1$, define the coefficients $a_j$ (depending on $k$) by the formal coordinate identity <a id="eq:permutation-delta-coordinate"></a>$$\exp\!\left(-\sum_{j\geq1}a_jx^{j+1}\frac{d}{dx}\right)x
       =\frac{(1+x)^k-1}{k},
 \label{eq:permutation-delta-coordinate}$$ and set <a id="eq:permutation-delta-operator"></a>$$\Delta_k(z)=\exp\!\left(\sum_{j\geq1}a_jz^{-j/k}L(j)\right)
                 k^{-L(0)}z^{(1/k-1)L(0)}.
 \label{eq:permutation-delta-operator}$$ These are the coordinate operators of <a id="pm-cite-80"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Section 5.1.1, equations (5.3)–(5.5)], with the chosen branches of the indicated powers.

<a id="eq:permutation-one-slot"></a>
Write $I_V$ and $I_D$ for the indicated spaces of ordinary and twisted intertwining operators, respectively. For ordinary $V$-modules $M,N$, the explicit mutually inverse maps of <a id="pm-cite-81"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Theorems 5.5, 5.9 and Corollary 5.10] give $$\begin{gathered}
 I_V\binom{W}{M\ N}
 \cong I_D\binom{T_\sigma(W)}{M\otimes V\ T_\sigma(N)},\\
 \overline{\mathcal Y}(m\otimes\mathbf1,z)
 =\mathcal Y(\Delta_2(z)m,z^{1/2}).
 \end{gathered}$$ Together with the BDM equivalence and the ordinary fusion universal property, this identifies the representing object: $$(M\otimes V)\star T_\sigma(N)
 \cong T_\sigma(M\boxtimes_V N).
 \label{eq:permutation-one-slot}$$ This is the universal-property proof of <a id="pm-cite-82"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Theorem 6.3], applied only to grading-restricted targets.

For completeness, the finite-expansion step in that proof does not require integral module weights. If $L(0)m=hm$, write $$e^{-\sum_{j\ge1}a_jz^{-j}L(j)}m=\sum_s z^{-s}m_s,
 \qquad L(0)m_s=(h-s)m_s.$$ The sum is finite by boundedness below, and $$\Delta_k(z^k)^{-1}m
 =z^{(k-1)h}\sum_s k^{h-s}z^{-ks}m_s.$$ In the weak-associativity substitution this fractional monomial depends only on $z_2$ and multiplies both sides. The corresponding expansion of a state $u\in V$ has integral powers. Taking a maximum over the finitely many coefficient pairs and absorbing their finitely many negative integral powers supplies the common exponent used in the proof of <a id="pm-cite-83"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Theorem 5.9].

To move the slot, pull back all three actions in an intertwiner by $\sigma$. This preserves twisted Jacobi and its universal property. For every ordinary $\sigma$-twisted object $Q$, $$E_Q=e^{-2\pi iL^Q(0)},\qquad
 E_Qa_nE_Q^{-1}=(-1)^j a_n
 \quad
 \bigl(\sigma a=(-1)^j a,\ n\in j/2+\mathbb Z\bigr).$$ Thus $E_Q:Q\longrightarrow Q^\sigma$ is an invertible module map. The charge pullback of $M\otimes V$ is $V\otimes M$. Applying simultaneous pullback to <a href="#eq:permutation-one-slot" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:permutation-one-slot">[eq:permutation-one-slot]</a> gives $$(V\otimes M)\star T_\sigma(N)
 \cong T_\sigma(M\boxtimes_V N).$$ This does not require $M\otimes V$ itself to be flip-invariant.

Finally, put $X=P\otimes V$, $Y=V\otimes P^\vee$. Ordinary tensor-product factorization and the vacuum unit give $X\star Y\cong P\otimes P^\vee$. Its factorized intertwiner is $$(p\otimes v,\ u\otimes q)\longmapsto
 e^{zL_P(-1)}Y_P(u,-z)p\otimes Y_{P^\vee}(v,z)q,$$ where $u$ belongs to the first factor and $v$ to the second. The relative associator and the two slot formulas now yield $$\begin{aligned}
 (P\otimes P^\vee)\star T_\sigma(V)
 &\cong (X\star Y)\star T_\sigma(V)\\
 &\cong X\star\bigl(Y\star T_\sigma(V)\bigr)\\
 &\cong (P\otimes V)\star T_\sigma(P^\vee)\\
 &\cong T_\sigma(P\boxtimes_V P^\vee).
 \end{aligned}$$ The dictionary permits writing $\boxtimes_D$ for these products. If $V=\mathbb C$, the flip is the identity and the statement is immediate. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-30"></a>
#### From detection to a positive invariant form

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-permutation-before-positivity</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:strong-unitarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:strong-unitarity">17</a>.* For $V=\mathbb C$, the only simple module is the scalar module, so the assertion is immediate. Assume henceforth that $V\ne\mathbb C$, and fix an arbitrary simple $V$-module $P$. Its algebraic contragredient is denoted by $P^\vee$; neither module is assumed unitary.

*The fixed-point algebra and a unitary twisted module.* Set $$D=V\otimes V,\qquad
 \sigma(v\otimes w)=w\otimes v,\qquad
 U=D^{\langle\sigma\rangle},\qquad J=D^{-\sigma}.$$ Thus $D=U\oplus J$ as a $U$-module. Tensor products preserve simplicity, regularity, CFT type and self-contragredience. In particular, $D$ is a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. The flip preserves its unitary form and commutes with its PCT operator $\Theta^D$. The cyclic fixed-point theorem of Carnahan–Miyamoto <a id="pm-cite-84"></a>[[CarnahanMiyamoto](#ref-CarnahanMiyamoto), Corollary 5.25] applies to $D$: it is simple, nonnegatively graded and regular, with a nonsingular invariant bilinear form. It follows that $U$ is regular. CFT type and the unitary structure are inherited, and the inherited form and PCT also give self-contragredience. Hence $U$ is simple unitary strongly rational, and Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a> applies to $U$.

Let $T=T_\sigma(V)$ be the transposition-twisted $D$-module obtained from the vacuum $V$ by the Barron–Dong–Mason construction <a id="pm-cite-85"></a>[[BDM](#ref-BDM), Theorem 3.9]. This construction preserves the unitary form <a id="pm-cite-86"></a>[[GuiPositivity](#ref-GuiPositivity), Lemma 10.12], so $T|_U$ is unitary. Its conformal energy is $$L_0^T=\tfrac12 L_0^V+\tfrac{c}{16},$$ where $c$ is the central charge of $V$. In particular, it has finite-dimensional energy spaces and a lower bound. Put $$R=P\otimes P^\vee.$$ The tensor-product module theorem makes $R$ simple over $D$; it too has finite-dimensional energy spaces and a lower bound.

The restrictions $T|_U$ and $R|_U$ have the same conformal vector and energy spaces as before restriction. They are grading-restricted $U$-modules of finite length. Indeed, regularity decomposes them into simple ordinary $U$-modules. There are finitely many simple types, and infinitely many copies of one type would make the energy space at its lowest weight infinite-dimensional. All fusion products over $U$ used below therefore belong to its finite semisimple module category.

<a id="eq:permutation-fusion-detection"></a>
*A positive form on the restriction of $R$.* Here $\boxtimes_D$ denotes the product representing twisted intertwining operators among grading-restricted ordinary or $\sigma$-twisted $D$-modules, as constructed in Lemma <a href="#lem:permutation-before-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:permutation-before-positivity">18</a>. That lemma gives $$\label{eq:permutation-fusion-detection}
 R\boxtimes_D T_\sigma(V)
 \cong T_\sigma(P\boxtimes_V P^\vee).$$ The ordinary labels $P$ and $P^\vee$ are still not assumed unitary. By rigidity, $P\boxtimes_V P^\vee$ has a nonzero evaluation onto $V$. Applying $T_\sigma$ to this morphism and using <a href="#eq:permutation-fusion-detection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:permutation-fusion-detection">[eq:permutation-fusion-detection]</a> yields a nonzero twisted intertwining operator $I$: the universal intertwiner’s coefficients generate its target, so composition with a nonzero target morphism cannot vanish. Its type is $$\binom{T}{R\ T}.$$

We need this operator to detect every simple summand of $R|_U$. For two weak $\sigma$-twisted $D$-modules, the space of generalized intertwining operators between them is a weak $D$-module <a id="pm-cite-87"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Theorem 4.6]; write $\mathcal H(T,T)$ for this space with source and target $T$. Moreover, <a id="pm-cite-88"></a>[[DLXY](#ref-DLXY), Proposition 4.8] identifies the charge map $$f_I:R\longrightarrow\mathcal H(T,T),\qquad p\longmapsto I(p,x),$$ as a homomorphism of weak $D$-modules. Its kernel is therefore a $D$-submodule. Since $R$ is simple and $I$ is nonzero, $f_I$ is injective.

Restricting the observable states in the twisted Jacobi identity to $U$ gives the ordinary Jacobi identity, with the same derivative identity and lower truncation. Thus $I$ is an ordinary $U$-intertwining operator. If $S\subset R|_U$ is any simple submodule, injectivity of $f_I$ implies that $I|_{S\times T}$ is nonzero. Rigidity over $U$ then gives a nonzero, hence injective, morphism $$S\longrightarrow T\boxtimes_U T^\vee.$$ The conjugate-module description of the contragredient gives a unitary form on $T^\vee$. Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>, applied to $U$, makes $T\boxtimes_U T^\vee$ unitary. Restricting its positive form unitarizes $S$. Taking the finite orthogonal sum over a simple decomposition of $R|_U$ gives a positive $U$-invariant form $h_1$ on $R$.

The form $h_1$ gives the correct adjoints for fields in $U$. It does not yet give the correct adjoints for fields in $J$, although those fields already act algebraically on $R$. We will construct a second positive form from their action and then add the two forms. Dong and Lin constructed unitary order-two simple-current VOA extensions under compatible antiunitary-involution hypotheses <a id="pm-cite-89"></a>[[DongLin](#ref-DongLin), Theorem 3.3]. Carpi, Gaudio, Giorgetti and Hillier proved unitarity and complete unitarity of simple CFT-type extensions of a completely unitary base <a id="pm-cite-90"></a>[[CGGH](#ref-CGGH), Theorem 4.7]. Here the extension algebra $D$ is already unitary, while the arbitrary module $R$ initially has a positive form only after restriction to $U$. The argument below transfers that form to the existing $D$-action.

*A second form from the sign action.* Since $V\ne\mathbb C$, the flip is faithful and $J\ne0$: if $v$ is independent of $\Omega$, then $v\otimes\Omega-\Omega\otimes v$ is a nonzero vector of $J$. The orbifold constituent theorem <a id="pm-cite-91"></a>[[McRaeOrbifold](#ref-McRaeOrbifold), Corollary 4.8] identifies the tensor subcategory generated by the constituents of $D$ with $\operatorname{Rep}(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)$. Here $D$ is an ordinary VOA, so the abelian associativity cocycle in that theorem is trivial. In particular, $J$ is a simple current, that is, a simple invertible object, and $$J\boxtimes_U J\cong U.$$ The multiplication morphism $$m:J\boxtimes_U J\longrightarrow U$$ coming from $Y^D|_{J\times J}$ is nonzero. For example, the invariant bilinear form of $D$ restricts nondegenerately to $J$, so some product of two states in $J$ has a nonzero vacuum coefficient. Thus $m$ is an isomorphism.

Let $$L:J\boxtimes_U R\longrightarrow R$$ be the morphism corresponding to the action $Y^R|_{J\times R}$. Ordinary $D$-module associativity, expressed using the fusing isomorphism over $U$, says that <a id="eq:sign-action-square"></a>$$\label{eq:sign-action-square}
 L(1_J\boxtimes L)\alpha_{J,J,R}
   =\ell_R(m\boxtimes1_R).$$ Here $\alpha_{J,J,R}:(J\boxtimes_U J)\boxtimes_U R
\longrightarrow J\boxtimes_U(J\boxtimes_U R)$ is the associator and $\ell_R:U\boxtimes_U R\longrightarrow R$ is the left unit isomorphism; undecorated fusion signs in this identity are over $U$. The right side is an isomorphism, so $L$ is epic. Tensoring by the invertible object $J$ preserves composition length. The two sides of $L$ have the same finite length, and hence $L$ is an isomorphism.

Give $J$ the form inherited from $D$. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a> to $J$ and $(R,h_1)$, and transfer its positive invariant fusion form along $L$. This gives a second positive $U$-invariant form $h_2$ on $R$, for which $Y^R|_{J\times R}$ is the transported universal intertwiner. We will show that $h_1+h_2$ is invariant for all of $D$.

<a id="eq:sign-annihilation"></a>
*Adjunction exchanges the two forms.* The creation intertwiner of the $U$-module $J$ is $Y^D|_{J\times U}$ by skew symmetry. With the inherited forms, its adjoint annihilation is $$\label{eq:sign-annihilation}
 \mathcal Y_J^-(\bar j,z)j_0
       =Y^D(\Theta^D j,z)j_0,
 \qquad j,j_0\in J.$$ This follows from unitarity of $D$ and fixes the scalar normalization of the annihilation. The linear identification $\bar J\cong J$ in <a href="#eq:sign-annihilation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sign-annihilation">[eq:sign-annihilation]</a> sends $\bar j$ to $\Theta^D j$.

<a id="eq:two-form-transport"></a>
For $w>0$, define the antilinear charge transform on $D$ by $$t_w=e^{wL_1}(-w^{-2})^{L_0}\Theta^D.$$ The integer grading of $D$ makes this expression unambiguous. For $j,j'\in J$ and finite-energy $r_1,r_2\in R$, the exact transport identity of Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>, followed by ordinary $D$-module associativity, gives $$\label{eq:two-form-transport}
\begin{aligned}
 &h_2\bigl(Y^R(j,z)r_1,Y^R(j',w)r_2\bigr)\\
 &\quad=h_1\bigl(
 Y^R(Y^D(t_wj',w^{-1}-z)j,z)r_1,r_2\bigr)\\
 &\quad=h_1\bigl(
 Y^R(t_wj',w^{-1})Y^R(j,z)r_1,r_2\bigr).
\end{aligned}$$ Initially, $0<z,w<1$ and $w^{-1}>z>w^{-1}-z>0$. Pairings of completed vectors on the first line are the absolutely convergent sums over equal energies supplied by the transport identity. On the last line, $h_1(\,\cdot\,,r_2)$ is a restricted-dual functional: $h_1$ is $U$-invariant and $U$ has the same conformal vector as $D$. Thus convergent module associativity applies without already assuming $D$-invariance of $h_1$.

<a id="eq:two-form-adjoint-one"></a>
The vectors $j_{(n)}r$, with $j\in J$, $r\in R$ and $n\in\mathbb Z$, span $R$. Their span is a $U$-submodule by the intertwiner commutator formula, and is the image of the surjective morphism $L$. Comparing Laurent coefficients in $z$ in <a href="#eq:two-form-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-form-transport">[eq:two-form-transport]</a> therefore yields $$\label{eq:two-form-adjoint-one}
 h_2\bigl(x,Y^R(j,w)y\bigr)
 =h_1\bigl(Y^R(t_wj,w^{-1})x,y\bigr),
 \qquad x,y\in R,\quad j\in J.$$ For finite-energy $x,y$, the two sides are finite Laurent polynomials in $w$. Indeed, the forms pair equal energies, the states of $D$ have integer weights, and $e^{wL_1}j$ is a finite sum. Consequently, <a href="#eq:two-form-adjoint-one" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-form-adjoint-one">[eq:two-form-adjoint-one]</a> extends from its nonempty initial interval to every $w>0$.

<a id="eq:charge-reflection-involution"></a>
<a id="eq:two-form-adjoint-two"></a>
The charge transform satisfies $$\label{eq:charge-reflection-involution}
 t_{w^{-1}}t_w=1.$$ For completeness, $\Theta^D$ commutes with the Virasoro operators and with the real integer-weight signs. Conjugating $e^{wL_1}$ by $(-w^2)^{L_0}$ gives $e^{-w^{-1}L_1}$, so $$t_{w^{-1}}t_w
 =e^{w^{-1}L_1}(-w^2)^{L_0}
   e^{wL_1}(-w^{-2})^{L_0}
 =1.$$ Apply <a href="#eq:two-form-adjoint-one" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-form-adjoint-one">[eq:two-form-adjoint-one]</a> at radius $w^{-1}$ with charge $t_wj$, exchange the two vector slots, and take complex conjugates. Using <a href="#eq:charge-reflection-involution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:charge-reflection-involution">[eq:charge-reflection-involution]</a> gives the reverse relation $$\label{eq:two-form-adjoint-two}
 h_1\bigl(x,Y^R(j,w)y\bigr)
 =h_2\bigl(Y^R(t_wj,w^{-1})x,y\bigr).$$ Adding <a href="#eq:two-form-adjoint-one" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-form-adjoint-one">[eq:two-form-adjoint-one]</a> and <a href="#eq:two-form-adjoint-two" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-form-adjoint-two">[eq:two-form-adjoint-two]</a> proves the invariant adjunction for $h=h_1+h_2$ on states of $J$. It already holds on $U$. Since $D=U\oplus J$, the form $h$ is $D$-invariant; it is positive definite as a sum of positive definite forms. Only ordinary associativity and the normalized transport identity were used in this construction.

Finally, choose a nonzero homogeneous vector $q\in P^\vee$ and put $$h_P(p,p')=h(p\otimes q,p'\otimes q),\qquad p,p'\in P.$$ This is positive definite. The fields of $V\otimes\Omega$ act on $P\otimes\mathbb Cq$ as the fields of $V$ on $P$, and the PCT charge transform on $v\otimes\Omega$ is the transform on $v$ tensored with $\Omega$. The $D$-invariant adjunction therefore restricts to the $V$-invariant adjunction for $h_P$. This unitarizes the arbitrary simple module $P$. Finite direct sums of these forms prove the final assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:comparison"></a>
### The canonical braided unitary tensor embedding

The preceding sections provide the analytic and positivity inputs for the categorical comparison. We now identify the tensor structure of the actual CWX functor. Essential surjectivity will be proved in the next section; at this stage we obtain an embedding and finite index for every sector in its image.

Here $\operatorname{Rep}(A_V)$ denotes the category of compatible families of locally normal representations of the interval algebras, with bounded operators intertwining every local action as morphisms. On the separable Hilbert completions considered here, these are the transportable DHR representations of the circle net; see <a id="pm-cite-92"></a>[[KLM](#ref-KLM), Section 6].

<a id="stmt-thm-cwx-embedding"></a>
<div id="thm:cwx-embedding" class="theorem">

**Theorem 19** (CWX tensor embedding). *Let $V$ be simple, unitary and strongly rational. The Hilbert completion and the local actions of Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a> define a fully faithful $*$-functor $$F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}:
 \operatorname{Rep}^u(V)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Rep}(A_V).$$ It has a unitary tensor structure identifying the Huang–Lepowsky fusion and braiding with Connes fusion and DHR braiding, respectively. In particular every object in its image is dualizable, hence has finite index.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-cwx-embedding"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-cwx-embedding</summary>

*Proof.* We first verify the analytic inputs to Gui’s comparison and identify the underlying functor. The additional localizability conditions will be checked before invoking the tensor comparison.

*Generating fields and charges.* We verify the hypotheses of Gui’s comparison theorem <a id="pm-cite-93"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Section 3.4 and Corollary 3.23]. Regularity follows from rationality and $C_2$-cofiniteness. Theorem <a href="#thm:strong-unitarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:strong-unitarity">17</a> supplies positive invariant forms on all simple modules; finite orthogonal sums then supply strong unitarity on the grading-restricted category. Theorem <a href="#thm:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:energy">2</a> gives energy bounds on every unitary module, and Corollary <a href="#cor:locality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:locality">5</a> gives strong locality of $V$.

Take a generating family $E$ of homogeneous quasi-primary vectors in $V$ as in the energy argument. Since strong locality holds for all vacuum fields, it holds in particular for $E$. Choose one representative of every simple module, equipped with its positive form, as the family of generating charges. This family certainly tensor-generates the algebraic category. In each charge module choose a nonzero lowest-weight vector $a$. It is quasi-primary because $L_1$ lowers conformal energy. Corollary <a href="#cor:charged-general" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:charged-general">16</a> and Lemma <a href="#lem:charged-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:charged-tools">10</a> give polynomial energy bounds for every intertwining operator between unitary modules with this charge. Proposition <a href="#prop:strong-intertwining" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:strong-intertwining">11</a> gives the strong intertwining property for every homogeneous observable, hence for $E$. The same fixed vector $a$ works for every irreducible intertwining operator with that charge; it has not been chosen separately for different sources or targets.

These are exactly Condition A of <a id="pm-cite-94"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Section 3.4]. The chosen charge vectors are quasi-primary, so Condition B holds as well. Thus <a id="pm-cite-95"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Theorem 3.15] identifies the canonical positive fusion structures with a unitary modular tensor category. This agrees with the normalization of Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>; positivity of an unspecified sesquilinear form is not being substituted for canonical positivity.

*The underlying fully faithful functor.* The functor on objects is the representation characterized by <a href="#eq:exact-cwx-smear" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-cwx-smear">[eq:exact-cwx-smear]</a>. On a module morphism it is its bounded extension to Hilbert completions. Such extensions are bounded because the modules are finite orthogonal sums of simple modules, and a map between simple unitary modules is either zero or a scalar multiple of a unitary isomorphism. The Hilbert adjoint is again a module map. The exact smear identity shows that these extensions intertwine the local algebra actions. They are therefore the morphism maps of the CWX functor, rather than of an independently chosen equivalence. Full faithfulness, including preservation of adjoints, is <a id="pm-cite-96"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Theorem 3.17]; see also <a id="pm-cite-97"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Theorem 4.24]. The uniqueness in Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a> identifies the functor there with ours. This full-faithfulness result uses the observable-smear argument of <a id="pm-cite-98"></a>[[GuiUnitaryII](#ref-GuiUnitaryII), Theorem 4.3]: localized stress-energy smears recover $L_0$ and then the algebraic module maps. It precedes the construction of a categorical extension and does not use tensor comparison.

*Localizability of finite sums and products.* We use the corrected localizability requirements in <a id="pm-cite-99"></a>[[GuiUnboundedErrata](#ref-GuiUnboundedErrata), item 3]. Here is their verification for the operators entering Gui’s categorical partial extension. Write $D_j=1+L_0$ on the Hilbert completion of a unitary module. The charged energy bounds and the $L_0$-commutator formula give, for each smeared field $T:\mathcal H_j^\infty\to\mathcal H_k^\infty$ and each nonnegative integer $s$, an integer $r_s$ and a constant $C_s$ such that $$\|D_k^sT\xi\|\leq C_s\|D_j^{r_s}\xi\|.$$ Indeed, commuting $L_0$ past a smear differentiates its smooth test and adds its fixed conformal-weight term; iteration reduces the estimate to the polynomial mode bounds. The same argument applies to the formal adjoint. This is the Sobolev estimate used in <a id="pm-cite-100"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Section 3.1 and Proposition 3.2].

Every bounded net morphism $S$ between objects of the CWX image is, by the full faithfulness just established, the bounded extension of a VOA morphism. All intermediate objects here are completions of VOA fusion products, on which the local actions are already defined. Thus $S$ and $S^*$ commute with the respective $L_0$ operators and preserve all Sobolev spaces. Gui’s left and right operators are products of a charged smear with finitely many observable smears <a id="pm-cite-101"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Equations (3.18)–(3.19)]. Their finite compositions, and those compositions followed by a bounded net morphism, therefore have the form covered by the preceding estimates. Consequently each finite sum, with common source and target, $$B=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{m}c_\alpha S_\alpha
       T_{\alpha,n_\alpha}\cdots T_{\alpha,1}
       \big|_{\mathcal H_j^\infty}$$ is smooth, has a densely defined formal adjoint, and satisfies $\|B\xi\|\leq C\|D_j^r\xi\|$ for some integer $r$. In particular $B$ is closable. This includes the finite sums of left or right fields in the corrected Definition 2.29 and the finite sums with a bounded morphism required by corrected Definition 2.41.

To verify localizability, let $\mathscr D_0\subset\mathcal H_j^\infty$ be a dense linear subspace that is quasi-rotation invariant: for some $\delta>0$ there is a dense $\mathscr D_\delta\subset\mathscr D_0$ such that $e^{itL_0}\mathscr D_\delta\subset\mathscr D_0$ for $|t|<\delta$. Then $\mathscr D_0$ is a core for $D_j^r$ <a id="pm-cite-102"></a>[[CKLW](#ref-CKLW), Lemma 7.2]. To see the regularization explicitly, choose $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(-\delta,\delta)$ and set $$R_\varphi=\int\varphi(t)e^{itL_0}\,dt.$$ The rapid decay of the Fourier transform of $\varphi$ makes $D_j^rR_\varphi$ bounded. Hence $R_\varphi$ turns Hilbert-norm approximations from $\mathscr D_\delta$ into Sobolev-norm approximations. For a vector in $\mathscr D_\delta$, its convolution is itself a Sobolev limit of Riemann sums in $\mathscr D_0$. Taking smooth approximate identities for $\varphi$ now approximates every vector of $\operatorname{Dom}D_j^r$ in that graph norm. The displayed bound for $B$ turns this approximation into graph approximation for $B$ on $\mathcal H_j^\infty$. Hence $\overline{B|_{\mathscr D_0}}=\overline B$, as required. Thus the strengthened finite-sum localizability hypotheses hold.

*Connes fusion, braiding, and finite index.* Full faithfulness alone would only allow us to transport the VOA tensor structure onto its image. The additional comparison with the already defined net tensor structure is <a id="pm-cite-103"></a>[[GuiUnbounded](#ref-GuiUnbounded), Corollary 3.23]. It supplies unitary natural maps $$U_{M,N}:F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(M)
       \boxtimes_{A_V}F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(N)
       \longrightarrow F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(M\boxtimes N),$$ compatible with the unit and associativity constraints. Here $\boxtimes_{A_V}$ denotes Connes fusion, while $\boxtimes$ denotes VOA fusion. Denote the VOA and net braidings by $c^V$ and $c^{\mathrm{DHR}}$, using the same circle orientation. The maps satisfy $$F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(c^{V}_{M,N})\,U_{M,N}
   =U_{N,M}\,
      c^{\mathrm{DHR}}_{F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(M),F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}(N)}.$$ Thus the comparison is braided and unitary for the specified fusion and braiding on the net side.

Finally, the source category is rigid. Applying this unitary tensor functor to its evaluation and coevaluation maps preserves the conjugate equations. Their images exhibit a dual for every object in the image. The equivalence between dualizability and finite index for the net sectors then gives the last assertion. ◻

</details>

The image is closed under finite sums and subobjects. For subobjects, full faithfulness lifts a net projection to a self-adjoint idempotent module endomorphism; its range is a unitary submodule, whose completion is the prescribed subrepresentation. This proves closure of the image, but does not yet rule out a simple sector outside it. The next section establishes precisely that exhaustion statement.

<a id="sec:exhaustion"></a>
### All sectors and complete rationality

The tensor functor constructed in Theorem <a href="#thm:cwx-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cwx-embedding">19</a> gives finite-index representations of the net. We must still show that it accounts for every finite sector. We prove the stronger statement that every irreducible locally normal representation comes from an ordinary unitary module. The point is to reconstruct a weak module before assuming that the representation has finite energy multiplicities. Regularity will then supply those multiplicities.

The scalar VOA has already been treated separately. We first consider $V\ne\mathbb C\Omega$. All representations of $A_V$ are understood to be compatible families of normal representations of its interval algebras.

<a id="stmt-prop-exhaustion"></a>
<div id="prop:exhaustion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 20**. *Let $V\ne\mathbb C\Omega$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA which is energy-bounded and strongly local. Every nonzero irreducible locally normal representation $(\mathcal K,\pi)$ of $A_V$ is the strong integration of an ordinary simple unitary $V$-module. No finite-index or finite-energy-multiplicity assumption on $\pi$ is required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-exhaustion"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-exhaustion</summary>

*Proof.* Write $A=A_V$. Reconstruction will give a weak module on the finite-energy vectors. We first establish the separability and discrete rotation spectrum needed for that construction; regularity will then produce a simple module whose Hilbert closure is the whole sector. The vacuum Hilbert space is separable, since it is the completion of the countable sum of the finite-dimensional spaces $V_n$. The Hilbert space $\mathcal K$ is also separable. To see this, choose a countable basis of intervals and, in the unit ball of each corresponding vacuum algebra, a countable $\sigma$-strong-\* dense subset. Such subsets exist because these von Neumann algebras have separable preduals. Normality and additivity imply that the images of these countably many subsets generate all the represented local algebras. The norm-closed unital \*-algebra they generate is separable and acts irreducibly. Every nonzero vector is therefore cyclic for this algebra, which proves the claim.

The canonical inner Möbius implementation in a locally normal representation has positive energy <a id="pm-cite-104"></a>[[Weiner](#ref-Weiner), Theorem 3.8]; its Virasoro action and the associated annulus action are the ones used in <a id="pm-cite-105"></a>[[HTVOA](#ref-HTVOA), Sections 2, 4 and 9]. Let $H\geq0$ be the self-adjoint generator of the lifted rotation group. Covariance implies that $e^{2\pi iH}$ commutes with every represented local algebra. Irreducibility makes it scalar.
