Thus, for some $h\in[0,1)$, the spectral theorem gives $$\operatorname{spec}H\subset (h+\mathbb Z)\cap[0,\infty).$$ In particular, $H$ has pure point spectrum. Its eigenspaces $\mathcal K_\lambda=\ker(H-\lambda)$ need not yet be finite dimensional. Set $$M=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\operatorname{spec}H}^{\mathrm{alg}}
        \mathcal K_\lambda.$$ This is a dense subspace of $\mathcal K$.

The VOA reconstructed from $A_V$ is $V$, with its given conformal vector and inner product <a id="pm-cite-106"></a>[[HTVOA](#ref-HTVOA), Theorem 8.9(ii)]. Here strong locality supplies AQFT-locality in the terminology of that theorem, and the required finite-dimensional vacuum energy spaces are precisely the spaces $V_n$. We now apply Proposition 9.3, Definition 9.4, Theorem 9.5 and Corollary 9.6 of <a id="pm-cite-107"></a>[[HTVOA](#ref-HTVOA)], together with Remark 9.8 of that paper. The remark extends all these conclusions to discrete rotation spectra with possibly infinite-dimensional eigenspaces. They provide a weak unitary $V$-module structure $Y^M$ on $M$ such that

1.  the conformal operator $L_0^M$ equals $H|_M$;

<a id="eq:sector-closed-fields"></a>
2.  for every homogeneous $a\in V$, smooth test function $f$, and interval $I$ containing its support, $$\label{eq:sector-closed-fields}
              \pi_I\!\left(\overline{Y(a,f)}\right)
                 =\overline{Y^M(a,f)|_M}.$$

On the left, a normal representation acts on an affiliated closed operator through its spectral and polar data. In particular, <a href="#eq:sector-closed-fields" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sector-closed-fields">[eq:sector-closed-fields]</a> asserts equality of closed operators, and $M$ is a core for the operator on either side. It does not assert only equality of formal matrix coefficients. This is the part of the reconstruction theorem needed below.

Strong rationality implies regularity: every weak $V$-module is an algebraic direct sum of ordinary simple modules <a id="pm-cite-108"></a>[[ABD](#ref-ABD), Theorem 4.5]. Choose a nonzero ordinary simple submodule $S\subset M$. Its inherited Hermitian form is positive and invariant. Because the two conformal operators agree, the homogeneous spaces $$S_\lambda=S\cap\mathcal K_\lambda$$ are finite dimensional. Let $Q$ be the orthogonal projection of $\mathcal K$ onto the Hilbert closure of $S$. Orthogonality of the energy spaces gives $$\overline S=\mathop{\widehat\bigoplus}_{\lambda}S_\lambda,
   \qquad Q(M)\subset S\subset M.$$ We will show that $Q$ commutes with the represented net. The domain argument is necessary here: algebraic invariance of $S$ alone would not give reduction of the closed smeared fields.

Both each mode and its algebraic adjoint preserve $S$. Indeed, the unitary adjoint formula expresses the latter as a finite linear combination of modes of PCT-transformed states and their $L_1$-descendants. Consequently $S^\perp\cap M$ is invariant under every mode, and $$Q a_{(n)}^M x=a_{(n)}^M Qx
       \qquad(a\in V,\ n\in\mathbb Z,\ x\in M).$$ The projection $Q$ also commutes with every energy projection. For $x\in M$, the component of $Y^M(a,f)x$ at a fixed energy is a finite sum of mode terms, since $x$ has only finitely many energy components. Comparing these components proves $$QY^M(a,f)x=Y^M(a,f)Qx\qquad(x\in M).$$ Both sides are Hilbert-space vectors by the reconstruction theorem, so componentwise equality suffices. The argument extends by finite sums to inhomogeneous $a$.

Put $T=\overline{Y^M(a,f)|_M}$. If $x\in\operatorname{Dom}T$, choose $x_j\in M$ with $x_j\to x$ and $Tx_j\to Tx$. Then $$Qx_j\longrightarrow Qx,
   \qquad TQx_j=QTx_j\longrightarrow QTx.$$ Closedness implies $Qx\in\operatorname{Dom}T$ and $TQx=QTx$. The same holds with $1-Q$ in place of $Q$. Hence the orthogonal decomposition defined by $Q$ reduces $T$, and $Q$ commutes with its bounded polar data. By <a href="#eq:sector-closed-fields" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sector-closed-fields">[eq:sector-closed-fields]</a>, these are the data of the represented vacuum smear. Such data generate $\pi_I(A(I))$, because vacuum smears generate $A(I)$ and $\pi_I$ is normal. Thus $Q$ commutes with every represented local algebra.

Irreducibility and $S\ne0$ now force $Q=1$. Each $S_\lambda$ is closed in $\mathcal K_\lambda$, so this identity implies $S_\lambda=\mathcal K_\lambda$ for every $\lambda$. Therefore $M=S$. In particular the energy multiplicities are finite and $M$ is an ordinary simple unitary module. Finally, <a href="#eq:sector-closed-fields" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sector-closed-fields">[eq:sector-closed-fields]</a> identifies its strongly integrated representation with $\pi$. The identification uses the actual closed smeared fields, as required by the CWX construction. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-thm-rationality"></a>
<div id="thm:rationality" class="theorem">

**Theorem 21**. *Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. Its conformal net $A_V$ is completely rational. The CWX functor constructed in Theorem <a href="#thm:cwx-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cwx-embedding">19</a> is essentially surjective onto the additive category of finite-index locally normal DHR representations of $A_V$. Consequently it is a braided unitary tensor equivalence $$F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}:
  \operatorname{Rep}^{u}(V)\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }
  \operatorname{Rep}^{f}(A_V).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-rationality"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-rationality</summary>

*Proof.* For the nontrivial VOA, Corollary <a href="#cor:locality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:locality">5</a> supplies the net and the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:exhaustion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exhaustion">20</a>. That proposition shows that every irreducible locally normal sector belongs to the CWX image. There are only finitely many such sectors, since $V$ has only finitely many simple modules. They have finite statistical dimension: Theorem <a href="#thm:cwx-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cwx-embedding">19</a> sends their rigid module duals and duality maps to net duals and duality maps. No finite-index assumption was used in Proposition <a href="#prop:exhaustion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exhaustion">20</a> to obtain this conclusion.

The irreducible diffeomorphism-covariant circle net $A_V$ has the split property by <a id="pm-cite-109"></a>[[MTW](#ref-MTW), Theorem 5.4]. The dichotomy <a id="pm-cite-110"></a>[[LongoXu](#ref-LongoXu), Theorem 4.9] applies to a split conformal net all of whose irreducible sectors have finite statistical dimension. It says that such a net is completely rational or has uncountably many inequivalent irreducible sectors. The finite list above excludes the second alternative. Thus $A_V$ is strongly additive and has finite $\mu$-index, as well as being irreducible and split.

For a locally normal representation on a separable Hilbert space, complete rationality gives a direct sum of irreducible sectors of finite statistical dimension <a id="pm-cite-111"></a>[[KLM](#ref-KLM), Corollary 39 and the proof of Corollary 40; Appendix B]. The modular-PCT hypothesis is automatic for conformal nets, as explained in Section 3, before Corollary 10, of <a id="pm-cite-112"></a>[[KLM](#ref-KLM)].

The same conclusion holds without separability. Indeed, apply the countable-generator construction from Proposition <a href="#prop:exhaustion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exhaustion">20</a> to the present representation $\pi$. The resulting separable unital C\*-algebra $D$ satisfies $$D''=\bigvee_I\pi_I(A_V(I)).$$ For any vector $\xi$, the separable cyclic space $\overline{D\xi}$ reduces $D$, hence also $D''$ and every represented interval algebra. A maximal orthogonal family of these cyclic reducing spaces exhausts the representation Hilbert space: any nonzero orthogonal complement would supply another such space. Compression to a reducing space preserves local normality. Apply the separable decomposition <a id="pm-cite-113"></a>[[KLM](#ref-KLM), Corollary 39 and the proof of Corollary 40; Appendix B] to each restriction and combine the resulting orthogonal sums.

In a dualizable representation only finitely many irreducible summands can occur. Any finite sub-sum of $n$ nonzero summands has statistical dimension at least $n$ and at most the dimension of the representation. This excludes infinite multiplicity even if the full decomposition has an uncountable index set. The CWX functor preserves finite direct sums, and Proposition <a href="#prop:exhaustion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exhaustion">20</a> supplies every irreducible summand. It is therefore essentially surjective onto $\operatorname{Rep}^{f}(A_V)$. Full faithfulness, the unitary tensor structure and compatibility with the DHR braiding were established in Theorem <a href="#thm:cwx-embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cwx-embedding">19</a>, so the functor is the asserted equivalence.

For $V=\mathbb C\Omega$, the scalar net has $\mu$-index one and its additive dualizable representation category is the category of finite-dimensional Hilbert multiplicity spaces. The same is true of finite-length unitary $V$-modules, with their ordinary tensor product and flip braiding. This gives the stated conclusion in the remaining case as well. ◻

</details>

Together with the energy bounds and strong locality of Theorem <a href="#thm:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:energy">2</a> and Corollary <a href="#cor:locality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:locality">5</a>, the unitarization and positive fusion forms of Theorems <a href="#thm:strong-unitarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:strong-unitarity">17</a> and <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a>, and the all-module integration of Theorem <a href="#thm:integration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integration">13</a>, this proves all five assertions of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Complete unitarity identifies $\operatorname{Rep}(V)$ with $\operatorname{Rep}^{u}(V)$; the equivalence just obtained is the specified CWX functor on this category, including finite direct sums.

The equivalence also gives a formula for the two-interval index. Let $M_1,\ldots,M_s$ represent the simple $V$-modules, with positive forms, and write $d(M_i)$ for their positive intrinsic categorical dimensions in $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V)$. A unitary tensor equivalence preserves these dimensions, so they equal the statistical dimensions of the corresponding net sectors. The two-interval index formula <a id="pm-cite-114"></a>[[KLM](#ref-KLM), Theorem 33] therefore gives $$\mu(A_V)=\sum_{i=1}^s d(M_i)^2.$$ Thus the index of the net is the global dimension of the VOA’s unitary fusion category.

<a id="sec:extensions"></a>
#### Finite-index local extensions

Gui’s comparison theorems now give an extension-level application of the base equivalence. We use the separable-representation and normalized extension conventions of <a id="pm-cite-115"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Definitions 3.9 and 5.1]: the VOA embedding is isometric and preserves the vacuum and conformal vector, while the net extension includes the specified isometric vacuum embedding and compatibility of the projective diffeomorphism actions.

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-index-extensions" class="corollary">

**Corollary 22** (Finite-index extension correspondence). *Let $V$ be a simple unitary strongly rational VOA. Up to isomorphism over the embedded base, $U\mapsto A_U$ gives a one-to-one correspondence between the following classes:*

1.  *normalized CFT-type unitary VOA extensions $V\hookrightarrow U$ whose underlying $V$-module is grading-restricted and of finite length;*

2.  *normalized irreducible local conformal-net extensions of $A_V$ of finite index.*

*The VOA class may equivalently be described by simple CFT-type algebraic extensions with the same conformal vector and finite-length underlying $V$-module, equipped with their unique unitary structure extending that of $V$.*

*Let $P$ be the normalized haploid commutative $C^*$-Frobenius algebra of $U$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(V)$, and let $\Theta$ be its transport to $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_V)$ under the base equivalence. The Q-system extension $B_\Theta$ equals $A_U$ as a conformal net on $\mathcal H_U$. Moreover, $A_U$ is completely rational and $$F^U_{\mathrm{CWX}}\circ F_{\mathrm{VOA}}
   =F_{\mathrm{CN}}\circ\widetilde F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}$$ as braided unitary tensor functors with Gui’s tensorators. Here $\widetilde F^V_{\mathrm{CWX}}$ transports unitary local (dyslectic) $P$-modules to local $\Theta$-modules, and $F_{\mathrm{VOA}}$ and $F_{\mathrm{CN}}$ are the canonical equivalences from these categories to $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^u(U)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_U)$, respectively. In particular, $F^U_{\mathrm{CWX}}$ is a braided unitary tensor equivalence onto all finite-index sectors of $A_U$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-index-extensions</summary>

*Proof.* If $V=\mathbb C\Omega$, the common zero conformal vector and CFT type force $U=\mathbb C\Omega$. A finite-index extension of the scalar net has finite-dimensional interval algebras. Since the interval factors of a nontrivial irreducible conformal net are type III <a id="pm-cite-116"></a>[[CKLW](#ref-CKLW), Section 3.3, property (7)], this extension is also scalar. The functors and tensorators then reduce to those of finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces. We may therefore assume $V\ne\mathbb C\Omega$.

Theorems <a href="#thm:positive-fusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-fusion">14</a> and <a href="#thm:strong-unitarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:strong-unitarity">17</a> together give complete unitarity, and Corollary <a href="#cor:charged-general" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:charged-general">16</a> gives energy bounds and strong intertwining for every irreducible unitary intertwining operator. These are Gui’s Condition I . Theorem <a href="#thm:rationality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rationality">21</a> adds complete rationality and essential surjectivity of the CWX functor, so $V$ satisfies Condition I+ of <a id="pm-cite-117"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions), Definition 6.17]. We use Gui’s Wassermann tensorator on the CWX functor; its underlying functor is the one identified in Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">8</a>.

Gui’s Theorems 3.13 and 5.3 classify the stated normalized net and unitary VOA extensions by normalized haploid commutative $C^*$-Frobenius algebras <a id="pm-cite-118"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions)]. A finite-index net extension has a dualizable Q-system object, hence one in $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}^f(A_V)$; every such object is a finite sum of VOA completions by Theorem <a href="#thm:rationality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rationality">21</a>, so is separable as required by Gui’s convention. The braided unitary base equivalence therefore transports all these algebra objects and their isomorphisms. For the algebraic VOA formulation, regularity from Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> and complete unitarity allow <a id="pm-cite-119"></a>[[CGGH](#ref-CGGH), Theorem 4.7], which gives the asserted unique unitary structure for simple CFT-type extensions.

Finally, Gui’s Corollary 6.5 and Theorem 6.18(2) pass Condition I+ to $U$. His Theorem 6.11 and Corollary 6.16 then give $A_U=B_\Theta$ and the displayed equality of braided unitary tensor functors, with the full finite-index sector category as target <a id="pm-cite-120"></a>[[GuiExtensions](#ref-GuiExtensions)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
#### References

<div class="thebibliography">

99
